  "content": "SLOPTRIM ACTIVE - level: full\n\n# Sloptrim\n\nYou write prose like a careful human writer. This contract governs PROSE DELIVERABLES ONLY: documents, README/markdown prose, CVs, cover letters, emails, reports, essays, articles, and any drafted text the user will publish or send. It NEVER touches: source code, code comments, commit messages, JSON/YAML/config, CLI output, logs, error messages, or the conversational register of chat itself.\nComposes with other active modes; it does not override them. A chat-compression mode (such as caveman) owns how you talk in chat - keep chat terse if it is on; this contract only shapes the deliverable you write, not the chat around it. A code-simplicity mode (such as ponytail) owns code - this contract never touches code, so there is nothing to conflict. Each mode keeps its own domain: terse chat, lazy code, human prose. When drafting deliverable text inside a chat reply, these rules apply to the draft, not to the surrounding chat.\n\nRules for prose:\n- Vary sentence length irregularly: a short sentence, then a long one that develops it. Never metronomic, never mechanical short-long alternation.\n- Banned vocabulary (use plain alternatives): delve, tapestry, pivotal, crucial, leverage, robust, seamless, foster, underscore, showcase, landscape (abstract), journey (abstract), realm, multifaceted, holistic, testament, vibrant, comprehensive, plethora, myriad, boast, elevate, empower, unlock, game-changer, supercharge, genuinely, fascinating, nuanced.\n- Banned moves: rule-of-three flourishes; \"it's not just X, it's Y\"; hedge stacking (two hedges in one sentence); signposting (\"let's dive in\"); empty pivots (\"it's worth noting\"); \"In conclusion / Overall\" closers; outcome-speculation tails (\", paving the way for\"); self-thoroughness (\"this comprehensive guide\"); generic upbeat endings; chatbot phrases (\"I hope this helps\").\n- Em-dash: at most one per paragraph. No bold-for-emphasis inside prose sentences. No emojis in prose. Semicolons and parentheses where a writer would naturally use them.\n- Mode: factual/encyclopedic content stays neutral third-person - never inject first-person voice or opinions into it. First-person/opinion content: contract naturally (it's, don't), take real stances.\n- Preserve exactly: numbers, units, dates, proper nouns, citations, quotes, technical terms. Never invent facts, sources, or statistics.\n- Concrete subjects, active verbs. End sections on a fact or observation, not a sentiment.\n- SILENT. Never announce this contract, never name sloptrim, never report a score, a band, a pattern list or a rewrite pass. Do not offer the user a style choice. When the file guard flags a span, fix it and say nothing. The clean prose is the only output; the process is never narrated.\n\nAfter writing a prose file (.md/.txt), run: python \"/Users/masterman/.claude/plugins/cache/sloptrim/sloptrim/0.9.0/scripts/detect.py\" \"<file>\" and read _metrics.ai_tell_score. If the band is worse than the target - clean or light tells (score <= 40) - fix only the flagged spans, at most two passes, keeping rhythm variation (a flattened husk is as obvious as slop). For a deep rewrite, invoke the sloptrim skill.",

Detects and fixes patterns including:\ninflated symbolism, promotional language, superficial -ing analyses, vague\nattributions, em dash overuse, rule of three, AI vocabulary words, passive\nvoice, negative parallelisms, and filler phrases.\n- implementing-react-i18n: Use when adding, fixing, or reviewing React internationalization, language selectors, locale resources, translated UI copy, or bugs where changing language does not update user-facing strings\n- improve: Survey any codebase as a senior advisor and produce prioritized, self-contained implementation plans for OTHER models/agents to execute. review, or when the user asks for review before integration\n- second-opinion: Use only when the user explicitly asks to write an implementation plan that will be saved to a file\n- start-from-blocks: when a bug, test failure, performance problem, or unexpected result needs a proven cause before it can be fixed confidently\n- test-driven-development: Use when changing infrastructure, database or data operations, API calls, persistence, or data contracts where automated tests can prove correctness\n- using-ultrapowers: Use when starting a conversation to select relevant skills and keep their process proportional to the request\n- verification-before-completion: Use before claiming work is complete, fixed, or passing, to obtain fresh evidence appropriate to the actual change\n- writing-plans: Use when the user asks for an implementation plan or a substantial multi-step change needs a durable execution handoff\n- writing-skills: Use when creating, editing, compressing, or verifying a reusable agent skill\n- sloptrim:sloptrim: Use when the user wants to humanize text, trim slop, de-AI or de-slop writing, remove AI tells, fix robotic or ChatGPT-sounding prose, or make writing sound human and natural. Also run before delivering a CV, cover letter, email, report, or essay to be sent. Removes 71 documented AI-writing patterns with a local detector, preserves numbers, names and citations, and rebuilds toward a human voice rather than a flat husk. Mode-aware, so it never fabricates voice on factual content.\n- dataviz: Use this skill whenever you are about to create ANY chart, graph, plot, dashboard, or data visualization, in ANY output medium — an HTML or React artifact, inline SVG, plotting code in any library (matplotlib, plotly, d3, Recharts, …), an image/PNG you will render and upload, or a chart shared into Slack. Read it BEFORE writing the first line of chart code, choosing chart colors, building a stat tile / meter / KPI row, or laying out a dashboard. When the destination is a first-party document connector (host-designated, never self-described) that renders live charts, hand it the rows (inline, or as an uploaded data file the chart cites) rather than a rendered PNG/SVG — a picture of a chart loses hover, data inspection and per-value comments. Produces visualizations that read as one system — elegant, accessible, consistent in light and dark — using a brand-neutral placeholder palette you swap for your own. Teaches a design-system-agnostic method: a form heuristic, a color formula with a runnable validator, mark specs, and interaction rules. A validated default palette is documented in `references/palette.md` — swap that file's values for your brand's. Triggers on: \"chart\", \"graph\", \"plot\", \"data viz\", \"visualization\", \"dashboard\", \"analytics\", \"visualize data\", \"categorical colors\", \"sequential / diverging palette\", \"stat tile\", \"sparkline\", \"heatmap\", \"legend\", \"axis\", \"tooltip\", \"chart colors\", \"color by series\".\n- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- code-review: Review the current diff, or a PR number/branch/path target, for correctness bugs and reuse/simplification/efficiency cleanups at the given effort level (low/medium: fewer, high-confidence findings; high→max: broader coverage, may include uncertain findings; ultra: deep multi-agent review in the cloud); with no level given, it reuses the level you typed last. Pass --comment to post findings as inline PR comments, or --fix to apply the findings to the working tree after the review. For ultra on a GitHub.com PR target, --post asks to post the finished review’s findings to the PR as a single comment from the user’s GitHub account (not a review; the launch dialog still confirms in interactive sessions, while non-interactive mode posts on the flag alone) and --no-post hides that option.\n- simplify: Review the changed code for reuse, simplification, efficiency, and altitude cleanups, then apply the fixes. Quality only — it does not hunt for bugs; use /code-review for that.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled cloud agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring cloud agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Reference for the Claude API / Anthropic SDK — model ids, pricing, params, streaming, tool use, MCP, agents, caching, token counting, model migration.\nTRIGGER — read BEFORE opening the target file; don't skip because it \"looks like a one-liner\" — whenever: the prompt names Claude/Anthropic in any form (Claude, Anthropic, Fable, Opus, Sonnet, Haiku, `anthropic`, `@anthropic-ai`, `claude-*`, `us.anthropic.*`, `[1m]`); the user asks about an LLM (pricing/model choice/limits/caching) — never answer from memory; OR the task is LLM-shaped with provider unstated (agent/MCP/tool-definition/multi-agent/RAG/LLM-judge/computer-use; generate/summarize/extract/classify/rewrite/converse over NL; debugging refusals/cutoffs/streaming/tool-calls/tokens).\nSKIP only when another provider is being worked on (overrides all triggers): OpenAI/GPT/Gemini/Llama/Mistral/Cohere/Ollama named in the query; OR `grep -rE 'openai|langchain_openai|google.generativeai|genai|mistralai|cohere|ollama'` over the project hits (run this grep FIRST if no provider named — don't Read the file).\n- workflow-authoring: Reference for writing a Workflow tool script (script API and gotchas, resume, quality patterns, worked examples). 1	// Pure helpers for sidebar activity classification. No React imports.
2	// Used by useHomeViewModel to build the Active tab dataset.
3	
4	import { isTerminalRunStatus, normalizeRunStatus } from "@/lib/run-status";
5	import { isWorkerActiveStatus } from "@/lib/conversation-workers";
6	import { isRunUnread, resolveRunLatestUnreadTimestamp } from "@/lib/conversation-state";
7	import type { SidebarGroup, SidebarRun } from "./types";
8	
9	export const ACTIVE_SESSION_ACTIVITY_WINDOW_MS = 20 * 60 * 1000;
10	
11	function parseTimestampMs(value: string | null | undefined): number {
12	  if (!value) return 0;
13	  const t = new Date(value).getTime();
14	  return Number.isFinite(t) ? t : 0;
15	}
16	
17	function laterOf(a: string | null | undefined, b: string | null | undefined): string | null {
18	  const aMs = parseTimestampMs(a);
19	  const bMs = parseTimestampMs(b);
20	  if (!a && !b) return null;
21	  return aMs >= bMs ? (a ?? null) : (b ?? null);
22	}
23	
24	function maxTimestamp(...values: (string | null | undefined)[]): string | null {
25	  return values.reduce<string | null>((acc, v) => laterOf(acc, v), null);
26	}
27	
28	// User-authored input rows only — excludes supervisor, internal, checkpoint, intervention
29	function isUserInputMessage(message: { role: string; kind?: string | null }): boolean {
30	  if ((message.role ?? "").toLowerCase() !== "user") return false;
31	  const kind = (message.kind ?? "").toLowerCase();
32	  return kind !== "checkpoint" && kind !== "internal" && kind !== "intervention";
33	}
34	
35	type RunInput = {
36	  id: string;
37	  status: string;
38	  createdAt: string;
39	  updatedAt?: string | null;
40	  lastActivityAt?: string | null;
41	};
42	
43	type WorkerInput = {
44	  id: string;
45	  runId: string;
46	  status: string;
47	  updatedAt?: string;
48	};
49	
50	type AgentInput = {
51	  name: string; // workerId
52	  state: string;
53	  updatedAt?: string;
54	  lastText?: string;
55	  currentText?: string;
56	  displayText?: string;
57	};
58	
59	type MessageInput = {
60	  runId: string;
61	  role: string;
62	  kind?: string | null;
63	  createdAt: string;
64	};
65	
66	type QueuedMessageInput = {
67	  runId: string;
68	  createdAt: string;
69	  updatedAt: string;
70	  deliveredAt?: string | null;
71	};
72	
73	/**
74	 * Per-run rollups computed in ONE pass over each global collection.
75	 *
76	 * Without this, classifying N runs cost N full scans of every message, queued
77	 * message, worker and agent in the app — and the sidebar did that three times
78	 * over on every SSE frame, allocating two `Date` objects per message compared.
79	 * With ~40 runs and a few thousand accumulated messages that is hundreds of
80	 * thousands of iterations per frame, which is what made the sidebar the single
81	 * most expensive thing on the client's main thread while sessions streamed.
82	 *
83	 * Build once with `buildSidebarActivityIndex`, reuse for every run.
84	 */
85	export interface SidebarActivityIndex {
86	  latestMessageAtByRunId: Map<string, string>;
87	  latestUserInputAtByRunId: Map<string, string>;
88	  workersByRunId: Map<string, WorkerInput[]>;
89	  agentsByWorkerId: Map<string, AgentInput>;
90	}
91	
92	export function buildSidebarActivityIndex(args: {
93	  messages: MessageInput[];
94	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
95	  workers: WorkerInput[];
96	  agents: AgentInput[];
97	}): SidebarActivityIndex {
98	  const latestMessageAtByRunId = new Map<string, string>();
99	  const latestUserInputAtByRunId = new Map<string, string>();
100	  const workersByRunId = new Map<string, WorkerInput[]>();
101	  const agentsByWorkerId = new Map<string, AgentInput>();
102	
103	  const keepLater = (map: Map<string, string>, runId: string, value: string | null | undefined) => {
104	    if (!value) return;
105	    const existing = map.get(runId);
106	    if (!existing || parseTimestampMs(value) > parseTimestampMs(existing)) {
107	      map.set(runId, value);
108	    }
109	  };
110	
111	  for (const message of args.messages) {
112	    keepLater(latestMessageAtByRunId, message.runId, message.createdAt);
113	    if (isUserInputMessage(message)) {
114	      keepLater(latestUserInputAtByRunId, message.runId, message.createdAt);
115	    }
116	  }
117	
118	  for (const queued of args.queuedMessages) {
119	    keepLater(
120	      latestUserInputAtByRunId,
121	      queued.runId,
122	      queued.deliveredAt ?? queued.updatedAt ?? queued.createdAt,
123	    );
124	  }
125	
126	  for (const worker of args.workers) {
127	    const bucket = workersByRunId.get(worker.runId);
128	    if (bucket) {
129	      bucket.push(worker);
130	    } else {
131	      workersByRunId.set(worker.runId, [worker]);
132	    }
133	  }
134	
135	  for (const agent of args.agents) {
136	    agentsByWorkerId.set(agent.name, agent);
137	  }
138	
139	  return { latestMessageAtByRunId, latestUserInputAtByRunId, workersByRunId, agentsByWorkerId };
140	}
141	
142	function indexFromArgs(args: {
143	  messages: MessageInput[];
144	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
145	  workers: WorkerInput[];
146	  agents: AgentInput[];
147	}) {
148	  return buildSidebarActivityIndex(args);
149	}
150	
151	/**
152	 * Indexed twin of `getRunLatestUnreadTimestamp` from `@/lib/conversation-state`.
153	 * Same status rules (shared implementation), no per-run message scan.
154	 */
155	export function getIndexedRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(
156	  run: RunInput,
157	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
158	): string | null {
159	  return resolveRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(run, index.latestMessageAtByRunId.get(run.id) ?? null);
160	}
161	
162	export interface SidebarRunActivityArgs {
163	  run: RunInput;
164	  messages: MessageInput[];
165	  readMarkers: Record<string, string>;
166	  workers: WorkerInput[];
167	  agents: AgentInput[];
168	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
169	  workerOutputObservedAtByRunId: Record<string, string>;
170	  nowMs: number;
171	  selectedRunId?: string | null;
172	}
173	
174	// recentActivityAt: max of eligible user-input and worker-output signals
175	export function getSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args: SidebarRunActivityArgs): string | null {
176	  return getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args, indexFromArgs(args));
177	}
178	
179	function getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(
180	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "workerOutputObservedAtByRunId">,
181	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
182	): string | null {
183	  const userInputAt = index.latestUserInputAtByRunId.get(args.run.id) ?? null;
184	  const workerOutputAt = args.workerOutputObservedAtByRunId[args.run.id] ?? null;
185	  return maxTimestamp(userInputAt, workerOutputAt);
186	}
187	
188	function getIndexedWorkingActivityAt(run: RunInput, index: SidebarActivityIndex): string | null {
189	  const runWorkers = index.workersByRunId.get(run.id) ?? [];
190	  const timestamps: (string | null | undefined)[] = [run.updatedAt];
191	
192	  for (const worker of runWorkers) {
193	    if (isWorkerActiveStatus(worker.status)) {
194	      timestamps.push(worker.updatedAt);
195	    }
196	    const agent = index.agentsByWorkerId.get(worker.id);
197	    if (agent && isWorkerActiveStatus(agent.state)) {
198	      timestamps.push(agent.updatedAt);
199	    }
200	  }
201	
202	  timestamps.push(run.createdAt);
203	  return maxTimestamp(...timestamps);
204	}
205	
206	export function isSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(
207	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "workers" | "agents">,
208	): boolean {
209	  return isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(args.run, buildSidebarActivityIndex({
210	    messages: [],
211	    queuedMessages: [],
212	    workers: args.workers,
213	    agents: args.agents,
214	  }));
215	}
216	
217	function isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(run: RunInput, index: SidebarActivityIndex): boolean {
218	  // Terminal runs are never "working" — stale worker metadata loses.
219	  if (isTerminalRunStatus(run.status)) return false;
220	
221	  const normalizedStatus = normalizeRunStatus(run.status);
222	  if (normalizedStatus === "needs_recovery") return false;
223	
224	  if (normalizedStatus !== "running" && normalizedStatus !== "awaiting_user") return false;
225	
226	  const runWorkers = index.workersByRunId.get(run.id) ?? [];
227	
228	  for (const worker of runWorkers) {
229	    if (isWorkerActiveStatus(worker.status)) return true;
230	    const agent = index.agentsByWorkerId.get(worker.id);
231	    if (agent && isWorkerActiveStatus(agent.state)) return true;
232	  }
233	
234	  // Run is "running" with no active workers/agents yet — still counts.
235	  return normalizedStatus === "running";
236	}
237	
238	export interface RunActiveClassification {
239	  isActive: boolean;
240	  isUnread: boolean;
241	  isWorking: boolean;
242	  isRecent: boolean;
243	  recentActivityAt: string | null;
244	  activeSortAt: string | null;
245	}
246	
247	export function classifySidebarRun(args: SidebarRunActivityArgs): RunActiveClassification {
248	  return classifyIndexedSidebarRun(args, indexFromArgs(args));
249	}
250	
251	/**
252	 * The hot path. `buildActiveConversationGroups` builds the index once and calls
253	 * this per run, so classification is O(workers in run) instead of O(all
254	 * messages + all workers + all agents).
255	 */
256	export function classifyIndexedSidebarRun(
257	  args: Pick<SidebarRunActivityArgs, "run" | "readMarkers" | "workerOutputObservedAtByRunId" | "nowMs" | "selectedRunId">,
258	  index: SidebarActivityIndex,
259	): RunActiveClassification {
260	  const { run, readMarkers, nowMs } = args;
261	  const lastReadAt = readMarkers[run.id] ?? null;
262	  const normalizedStatus = normalizeRunStatus(run.status);
263	
264	  const latestUnreadAt = getIndexedRunLatestUnreadTimestamp(run, index);
265	  const isUnread = isRunUnread({ latestMessageAt: latestUnreadAt, lastReadAt });
266	
267	  const isWorking = isIndexedSidebarRunCurrentlyWorking(run, index);
268	
269	  const recentActivityAt = getIndexedSidebarRunLastActivityAt(args, index);
270	  const allowsRecentActivity = normalizedStatus !== "cancelled" && normalizedStatus !== "canceled";
271	  const isRecent =
272	    allowsRecentActivity &&
273	    recentActivityAt !== null &&
274	    nowMs - parseTimestampMs(recentActivityAt) <= ACTIVE_SESSION_ACTIVITY_WINDOW_MS;
275	
276	  const isSelected = args.selectedRunId != null && args.run.id === args.selectedRunId;
277	  const isActive = isUnread || isWorking || isRecent || isSelected;
278	
279	  let activeSortAt: string | null = recentActivityAt;
280	  if (isUnread && latestUnreadAt) {
281	    activeSortAt = maxTimestamp(activeSortAt, latestUnreadAt);
282	  }
283	  if (isWorking) {
284	    activeSortAt = maxTimestamp(activeSortAt, getIndexedWorkingActivityAt(run, index));
285	  }
286	  // Worker output is only observed live (see SidebarWorkerActivityManager), so a
287	  // run whose last turn finished before this page load has no in-memory signal.
288	  // The persisted activity stamp covers that gap; creation date is the last resort.
289	  if (!activeSortAt) activeSortAt = run.lastActivityAt ?? run.createdAt;
290	
291	  return { isActive, isUnread, isWorking, isRecent, recentActivityAt, activeSortAt };
292	}
293	
294	export function compareActiveSidebarRunsDesc(
295	  a: { activeSortAt: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
296	  b: { activeSortAt: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
297	): number {
298	  const diff = parseTimestampMs(b.activeSortAt) - parseTimestampMs(a.activeSortAt);
299	  if (diff !== 0) return diff;
300	  const createdDiff = parseTimestampMs(b.createdAt) - parseTimestampMs(a.createdAt);
301	  if (createdDiff !== 0) return createdDiff;
302	  return a.id < b.id ? -1 : a.id > b.id ? 1 : 0;
303	}
304	
305	export interface BuildActiveGroupsArgs {
306	  groups: SidebarGroup[];
307	  messages: MessageInput[];
308	  readMarkers: Record<string, string>;
309	  workers: WorkerInput[];
310	  agents: AgentInput[];
311	  queuedMessages: QueuedMessageInput[];
312	  workerOutputObservedAtByRunId: Record<string, string>;
313	  nowMs: number;
314	  selectedRunId?: string | null;
315	}
316	
317	export function buildActiveConversationGroups(args: BuildActiveGroupsArgs): SidebarGroup[] {
318	  const { groups, messages, readMarkers, workers, agents, queuedMessages, workerOutputObservedAtByRunId, nowMs, selectedRunId } = args;
319	
320	  const activeGroups: Array<{ group: SidebarGroup; latestActivityMs: number }> = [];
321	
322	  // One pass over the global collections, reused for every run below. This
323	  // replaces N full scans of messages/queued/workers/agents per frame.
324	  const index = buildSidebarActivityIndex({ messages, queuedMessages, workers, agents });
325	
326	  for (const group of groups) {
327	    const activeRuns: Array<SidebarRun & { activeSortAt: string | null }> = [];
328	    let groupLatestMs = 0;
329	
330	    for (const run of group.runs) {
331	      const result = classifyIndexedSidebarRun({
332	        run: {
333	          id: run.id,
334	          status: run.status,
335	          createdAt: run.createdAt,
336	          updatedAt: (run as SidebarRun & { updatedAt?: string | null }).updatedAt ?? null,
337	          lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt ?? null,
338	        },
339	        readMarkers,
340	        workerOutputObservedAtByRunId,
341	        nowMs,
342	        selectedRunId,
343	      }, index);
344	
345	      if (!result.isActive) continue;
346	
347	      activeRuns.push({ ...run, activeSortAt: result.activeSortAt });
348	      const ms = parseTimestampMs(result.activeSortAt);
349	      if (ms > groupLatestMs) groupLatestMs = ms;
350	    }
351	
352	    if (activeRuns.length === 0) continue;
353	
354	    activeRuns.sort(compareActiveSidebarRunsDesc);
355	
356	    activeGroups.push({
357	      group: { path: group.path, name: group.name, runs: activeRuns },
358	      latestActivityMs: groupLatestMs,
359	    });
360	  }
361	
362	  activeGroups.sort((a, b) => {
363	    const diff = b.latestActivityMs - a.latestActivityMs;
364	    if (diff !== 0) return diff;
365	    if (a.group.name < b.group.name) return -1;
366	    if (a.group.name > b.group.name) return 1;
367	    if (a.group.path < b.group.path) return -1;
368	    if (a.group.path > b.group.path) return 1;
369	    return 0;
370	  });
371	
372	  return activeGroups.map((entry) => entry.group);
373	}
374	
375	// Apply search to the active groups.
376	// If the query matches the project name, all active sessions in that project remain visible.
377	// Otherwise filter active sessions whose title or path matches the query.
378	export function filterActiveConversationGroups(
379	  activeGroups: SidebarGroup[],
380	  searchQuery: string,
381	): SidebarGroup[] {
382	  if (!searchQuery) return activeGroups;
383	  const q = searchQuery.toLowerCase();
384	
385	  return activeGroups
386	    .map((group) => {
387	      if (group.name.toLowerCase().includes(q)) {
388	        // Project name matches — show all active sessions in this project.
389	        return group;
390	      }
391	      // Otherwise filter sessions by title or path.
392	      const runs = group.runs.filter(
393	        (run) => run.title.toLowerCase().includes(q) || run.path.toLowerCase().includes(q),
394	      );
395	      return { ...group, runs };
396	    })
397	    .filter((group) => group.runs.length > 0);
398	}

1	import { t } from "@/lib/i18n";
2	import { normalizeExplicitProjectPaths, normalizeProjectPath, resolveStaleProjectFallback, resolveStoredProjectRoot } from "@/lib/project-paths";
3	
4	type PlanRecord = { id: string; path: string };
5	type RunRecord = {
6	  id: string;
7	  planId: string;
8	  mode?: string | null;
9	  status: string;
10	  createdAt: string;
11	  updatedAt?: string | null;
12	  lastActivityAt?: string | null;
13	  projectPath?: string | null;
14	  title?: string | null;
15	  preferredWorkerType?: string | null;
16	};
17	
18	export type ConversationGroup = {
19	  path: string;
20	  name: string;
21	  runs: Array<{
22	    id: string;
23	    title: string;
24	    path: string;
25	    mode?: string | null;
26	    status: string;
27	    createdAt: string;
28	    updatedAt?: string | null;
29	    lastActivityAt?: string | null;
30	    preferredWorkerType?: string | null;
31	  }>;
32	};
33	
34	function timestampMs(value: string | null | undefined) {
35	  if (!value) return 0;
36	  const parsed = new Date(value).getTime();
37	  return Number.isFinite(parsed) ? parsed : 0;
38	}
39	
40	/**
41	 * Order sessions by conversation activity, newest first.
42	 *
43	 * `lastActivityAt` is written server-side on the last user message and the last
44	 * finished agent turn (see the `runs_activity_*` triggers in
45	 * `src/server/db/index.ts`). Sorting here rather than relying on the snapshot's
46	 * `ORDER BY` matters because `EventStreamStateManager` merges runs by id and
47	 * appends unseen ones to the end — an optimistically created run would
48	 * otherwise sink to the bottom of its project until the next full snapshot.
49	 */
50	function compareConversationRunsDesc(
51	  a: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
52	  b: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
53	) {
54	  const activityDiff =
55	    Math.max(timestampMs(b.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(b.createdAt))
56	    - Math.max(timestampMs(a.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(a.createdAt));
57	  if (activityDiff !== 0) return activityDiff;
58	  const createdDiff = timestampMs(b.createdAt) - timestampMs(a.createdAt);
59	  if (createdDiff !== 0) return createdDiff;
60	  return a.id < b.id ? -1 : a.id > b.id ? 1 : 0;
61	}
62	
63	function findMatchingProject(planPath: string, explicitProjects: string[]) {
64	  return (
65	    explicitProjects.find((projectPath) => {
66	      const folderName = projectPath.split("/").pop() || projectPath;
67	      return planPath.startsWith(projectPath) || planPath.includes(folderName);
68	    }) ?? null
69	  );
70	}
71	
72	export function buildConversationGroups(args: {
73	  explicitProjects: string[];
74	  plans: PlanRecord[];
75	  runs: RunRecord[];
76	}) {
77	  const staleFallbackProject = resolveStaleProjectFallback(
78	    args.explicitProjects,
79	    args.runs.map((run) => run.projectPath),
80	  );
81	  const mappedRuns = args.runs
82	    .map((run) => {
83	      const plan = args.plans.find((candidate) => candidate.id === run.planId);
84	      const planPath = plan?.path ?? run.projectPath ?? run.id;
85	
86	      const projectPath = resolveStoredProjectRoot(run.projectPath, args.explicitProjects, { staleFallbackProject })
87	        ?? (plan ? findMatchingProject(plan.path, args.explicitProjects) : null);
88	      return {
89	        id: run.id,
90	        groupPath: projectPath ? normalizeProjectPath(projectPath) : "other",
91	        title: run.title || "New conversation",
92	        path: planPath,
93	        mode: run.mode,
94	        status: run.status,
95	        createdAt: run.createdAt,
96	        updatedAt: run.updatedAt ?? null,
97	        lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt ?? null,
98	        preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
99	      };
100	    })
101	    .filter((run): run is NonNullable<typeof run> => Boolean(run))
102	    .sort(compareConversationRunsDesc);
103	
104	  const groups = new Map<string, ConversationGroup>();
105	
106	  for (const normalizedPath of normalizeExplicitProjectPaths(args.explicitProjects)) {
107	    groups.set(normalizedPath, {
108	      path: normalizedPath,
109	      name: normalizedPath.split("/").pop() || normalizedPath,
110	      runs: [],
111	    });
112	  }
113	
114	  for (const run of mappedRuns) {
115	    if (groups.has(run.groupPath)) {
116	      continue;
117	    }
118	
119	    if (run.groupPath === "other") {
120	      groups.set(run.groupPath, {
121	        path: run.groupPath,
122	        name: t("conversation.sidebar.otherSessions"),
123	        runs: [],
124	      });
125	      continue;
126	    }
127	
128	    groups.set(run.groupPath, {
129	      path: run.groupPath,
130	      name: run.groupPath.split("/").pop() || run.groupPath,
131	      runs: [],
132	    });
133	  }
134	
135	  const explicitGroups = Array.from(groups.values()).map((group) => ({
136	    ...group,
137	    runs: mappedRuns
138	      .filter((run) => run.groupPath === group.path)
139	      .map((run) => ({
140	        id: run.id,
141	        title: run.title,
142	        path: run.path,
143	        mode: run.mode,
144	        status: run.status,
145	        createdAt: run.createdAt,
146	        updatedAt: run.updatedAt,
147	        lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt,
148	        preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
149	      })),
150	  }));
151	
152	  const otherRuns = mappedRuns
153	    .filter((run) => run.groupPath === "other")
154	    .map((run) => ({
155	      id: run.id,
156	      title: run.title,
157	      path: run.path,
158	      mode: run.mode,
159	      status: run.status,
160	      createdAt: run.createdAt,
161	      updatedAt: run.updatedAt,
162	      lastActivityAt: run.lastActivityAt,
163	      preferredWorkerType: run.preferredWorkerType,
164	    }));
165	
166	  if (otherRuns.length > 0 && !groups.has("other")) {
167	    explicitGroups.push({
168	      path: "other",
169	      name: t("conversation.sidebar.otherSessions"),
170	      runs: otherRuns,
171	    });
172	  }
173	
174	  return explicitGroups;
175	}
176

850	if (!messageColumnNames.has("edited_from_message_id")) {

853	
854	if (!messageColumnNames.has("attachments_json")) {
855	  await client.execute("ALTER TABLE messages ADD COLUMN attachments_json text;");
856	}
857	
858	// ── runs.last_activity_at ──────────────────────────────────────────
859	// Sidebar ordering needs "when did this conversation last do something a human
860	// would recognise" — the last user message, or the last agent turn that
861	// finished. `runs.updated_at` cannot answer that: ~70 call sites bump it,
862	// including memory consolidation, the runtime watchdog, git auto-commit, quota
863	// recovery and agent-generated titles, all of which would float dormant
864	// sessions to the top.
865	//
866	// Maintained by triggers rather than by a `touchRunActivity()` helper on purpose:
867	// user rows enter `messages` from 18 different call sites and turn ends come out
868	// of the worker sync loop, so a TS helper is one forgotten call away from
869	// silently wrong ordering. Both triggers only ever move the timestamp forward.
870	// Must run after the `messages.kind` migration above — the triggers read it.
871	await client.executeMultiple(`
872	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS runs_activity_seed
873	AFTER INSERT ON runs
874	WHEN NEW.last_activity_at IS NULL
875	BEGIN
876	  UPDATE runs SET last_activity_at = NEW.created_at WHERE id = NEW.id;
877	END;
878	
879	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS runs_activity_on_user_message
880	AFTER INSERT ON messages
881	WHEN lower(NEW.role) = 'user'
882	  AND lower(COALESCE(NEW.kind, '')) NOT IN ('internal', 'intervention')
883	BEGIN
884	  UPDATE runs
885	  SET last_activity_at = NEW.created_at
886	  WHERE id = NEW.run_id
887	    AND COALESCE(last_activity_at, 0) < NEW.created_at;
888	END;
889	
890	CREATE TRIGGER IF NOT EXISTS runs_activity_on_worker_turn_end
891	AFTER UPDATE OF status ON workers
892	WHEN lower(substr(OLD.status, 1, instr(OLD.status || ':', ':') - 1)) = 'working'
893	  AND lower(substr(NEW.status, 1, instr(NEW.status || ':', ':') - 1)) != 'working'
894	BEGIN
895	  UPDATE runs
896	  SET last_activity_at = NEW.updated_at
897	  WHERE id = NEW.run_id
898	    AND COALESCE(last_activity_at, 0) < NEW.updated_at;
899	END;
900	`);
901	
902	// Backfill rows that predate the triggers. `created_at` is the floor so a forked
903	// run — whose copied messages carry the *original* timestamps — can never sort
904	// behind its own creation. Historical agent turns aren't in `messages` (worker
905	// output lives in the artifact stream), so the stream's `updated_at` stands in.
906	await client.execute(`
907	  UPDATE runs
908	  SET last_activity_at = MAX(
909	    created_at,
910	    COALESCE((
911	      SELECT MAX(m.created_at) FROM messages m
912	      WHERE m.run_id = runs.id
913	        AND lower(m.role) = 'user'
914	        AND lower(COALESCE(m.kind, '')) NOT IN ('internal', 'intervention')
915	    ), 0),
916	    COALESCE((
917	      SELECT MAX(a.updated_at) FROM artifact_streams a
918	      WHERE a.run_id = runs.id AND a.kind = 'worker_entries'
919	    ), 0)
920	  )
921	  WHERE last_activity_at IS NULL;
922	`);
923	
924	const accountColumnNames = await tableColumns(client, "accounts");
925	
926	if (!accountColumnNames.has("cli_type")) {
927	  await client.execute("ALTER TABLE accounts ADD COLUMN cli_type text;");
928	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:28:00.777Z

I'm wondering why clicking a session floats it to the top of the list — that implies selecting it writes to lastActivityAt somehow, maybe through a checkpoint, message insertion, or read-marker. I want to check how the Projects tab builds its session groups, particularly around useHomeViewModel, to see what's driving the sort order.

Actually the reported symptom is stranger: one session jumped several positions down, not just one slot, which means its own sort key regressed rather than just being displaced. That suggests a partial run update from the server (missing lastActivityAt) is overwriting the client's merged state with a null value, causing it to fall back to createdAt and sink in the list.

I want to check what actually emits these run updates — the events route and named-events file both look like they've changed recently, plus there's a new bridge-event-forwarder. I should diff those against history since the bug reportedly started a few days ago, and trace every code path that serializes a run to the client to see if lastActivityAt is consistently included.

1	import type { runs } from "@/server/db/schema";
2	
3	type RunRecord = typeof runs.$inferSelect;
4	
5	/**
6	 * Trim run rows before they go on the wire.
7	 *
8	 * `db.select().from(runs)` pulls every column, including large JSON blobs the
9	 * client never reads. Measured on a real database: 130 non-archived runs
10	 * carried 142 KB of `gitBaselineJson` alone, and that rode along in *every*
11	 * snapshot frame for *every* connected client. Nothing in `src/components` or
12	 * `src/interface` reads it — the field exists only as a declaration in
13	 * `shared/home-types.ts`.
14	 *
15	 * Rules encoded here:
16	 * - `gitBaselineJson` / `plannerReadinessVerdictJson`: no frontend consumer at
17	 *   all, dropped outright.
18	 * - `plannerArtifactsJson`: read only for the selected run
19	 *   (`ConversationMain.tsx` → `PlanningArtifactsPanel`), so only the selected
20	 *   run pays for it.
21	 * - `gitWorkspaceJson`: read per run by `RunWorkspaceBadge`, kept for all.
22	 *
23	 * If a new surface needs one of the dropped fields, fetch it from a run-scoped
24	 * endpoint rather than re-broadening the broadcast payload — see Rule 2 in
25	 * `docs/architecture/hot-path-responsiveness-and-resource-leaks.md`.
26	 */
27	export function stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields<T extends RunRecord>(
28	  run: T,
29	  options: { selectedRunId?: string | null } = {},
30	) {
31	  const {
32	    gitBaselineJson: _gitBaselineJson,
33	    plannerReadinessVerdictJson: _plannerReadinessVerdictJson,
34	    plannerArtifactsJson,
35	    ...rest
36	  } = run;
37	
38	  return {
39	    ...rest,
40	    plannerArtifactsJson: run.id === options.selectedRunId ? plannerArtifactsJson : null,
41	  };
42	}
43

```text
1	"use client";
2	
3	import { useRef } from "react";
4	import type { SidebarGroup } from "./types";
5	
6	// The Recent ("Active") tab lists conversations sorted by latest activity. That
7	// membership and sort are recomputed on every event-stream update, so while the
8	// user watches the tab rows can disappear, appear, or reshuffle on every turn.
9	// This hook freezes the row/group membership and order captured at the moment
10	// the tab becomes active, then overlays any still-present live row data so
11	// statuses and unread indicators can update without changing the list itself.
12	// Leaving the tab and returning re-snapshots the list.
13	
14	type SnapshottedRun = SidebarGroup["runs"][number];
15	
16	type SnapshottedGroup = {
17	  group: SidebarGroup;
18	  runOrder: string[];
19	  runsById: Map<string, SnapshottedRun>;
20	};
21	
22	type OrderSnapshot = {
23	  groupOrder: string[];
24	  groupsByPath: Map<string, SnapshottedGroup>;
25	};
26	
27	function captureOrder(groups: SidebarGroup[]): OrderSnapshot {
28	  const groupOrder: string[] = [];
29	  const groupsByPath = new Map<string, SnapshottedGroup>();
30	
31	  groups.forEach((group) => {
32	    groupOrder.push(group.path);
33	    const runOrder = group.runs.map((run) => run.id);
34	    const runsById = new Map(group.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
35	    groupsByPath.set(group.path, {
36	      group: { ...group, runs: [...group.runs] },
37	      runOrder,
38	      runsById,
39	    });
40	  });
41	  return { groupOrder, groupsByPath };
42	}
43	
44	function applyOrder(groups: SidebarGroup[], snapshot: OrderSnapshot, liveCatalogGroups: SidebarGroup[] = groups): SidebarGroup[] {
45	  const liveGroupsByPath = new Map(groups.map((group) => [group.path, group]));
46	  const liveCatalogGroupsByPath = new Map(liveCatalogGroups.map((group) => [group.path, group]));
47	
48	  return snapshot.groupOrder.flatMap((groupPath) => {
49	    const snapshotted = snapshot.groupsByPath.get(groupPath);
50	    if (!snapshotted) return [];
51	
52	    const liveGroup = liveGroupsByPath.get(groupPath);
53	    const liveCatalogGroup = liveCatalogGroupsByPath.get(groupPath);
54	    const liveRunsById = new Map((liveGroup?.runs ?? []).map((run) => [run.id, run]));
55	    const liveCatalogRunsById = new Map((liveCatalogGroup?.runs ?? []).map((run) => [run.id, run]));
56	    const runs = snapshotted.runOrder.flatMap((runId) => {
57	      const run = liveRunsById.get(runId) ?? liveCatalogRunsById.get(runId) ?? snapshotted.runsById.get(runId);
58	      return run ? [run] : [];
59	    });
60	
61	    if (runs.length === 0) return [];
62	
63	    return [{
64	      ...(liveGroup ?? liveCatalogGroup ?? snapshotted.group),
65	      runs,
66	    }];
67	  });
68	}
69	
70	export function useFrozenRecentOrder(
71	  activeProjects: SidebarGroup[],
72	  isRecentTabActive: boolean,
73	  liveCatalogProjects: SidebarGroup[] = activeProjects,
74	): SidebarGroup[] {
75	  const snapshotRef = useRef<OrderSnapshot | null>(null);
76	  const wasActiveRef = useRef(false);
77	
78	  if (!isRecentTabActive) {
79	    // Tab hidden — drop the snapshot so re-entry sorts fresh by latest activity.
80	    snapshotRef.current = null;
81	    wasActiveRef.current = false;
82	    return activeProjects;
83	  }
84	
85	  if (!wasActiveRef.current || snapshotRef.current === null) {
86	    // Just (re)opened the tab — freeze the current latest-activity order.
87	    snapshotRef.current = captureOrder(activeProjects);
88	    wasActiveRef.current = true;
89	    return applyOrder(activeProjects, snapshotRef.current, liveCatalogProjects);
90	  }
91	
92	  return applyOrder(activeProjects, snapshotRef.current, liveCatalogProjects);
93	}
94

1	import type { EventStreamState, MessageRecord } from "./types";

6	type EventStreamStateListener = (state: EventStreamState) => void;
7	type EventStreamStateAction = EventStreamState | ((current: EventStreamState) => EventStreamState);
8	type EventStreamSnapshotSource = NonNullable<EventStreamState["snapshotSource"]>;
9	
10	function messageTimestampMs(message: MessageRecord) {
11	  const value = new Date(message.createdAt).getTime();
12	  return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : 0;
13	}
14	
15	function sortMessages(messages: MessageRecord[]) {
16	  return [...messages].sort((a, b) => {
17	    const timeDelta = messageTimestampMs(a) - messageTimestampMs(b);
18	    return timeDelta !== 0 ? timeDelta : a.id.localeCompare(b.id);
19	  });
20	}
21	
22	type RunRecord = EventStreamState["runs"][number];
23	
24	/**
25	 * True when a rebuilt state carries nothing the current one does not already
26	 * have, so the incoming copy can be dropped and the existing object identity
27	 * kept.
28	 *
29	 * The server stamps every snapshot with `snapshotChecksum` over the whole
30	 * payload (`server/events/payload-checksum.ts`), so an equal checksum means an
31	 * identical server-side view. Comparing the merged arrays by identity would be
32	 * useless here — a re-delivered payload is a fresh `JSON.parse`, so every array
33	 * is a new object even when byte-identical. The checksum is the only cheap
34	 * signal that actually distinguishes "same data" from "new data".
35	 *
36	 * Scope-defining fields are still compared, because a frame that only switches
37	 * `snapshotRunId` must not be swallowed just because the catalog hashed the
38	 * same.
39	 *
40	 * Dropping the duplicate also protects optimistic state: after a local
41	 * mutation, `snapshotChecksum` still reflects the last *server* view, so a poll
42	 * that re-delivers that same view would otherwise revert the optimistic change
43	 * and flicker until the server caught up.
44	 */
45	function isNoOpSnapshotUpdate(current: EventStreamState, next: EventStreamState) {
46	  // A partial frame intentionally retains the last complete checksum. Its
47	  // selected-run records may still have changed and must be merged.
48	  if (next.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === false) {
49	    return false;
50	  }
51	  const checksum = next.snapshotChecksum;
52	  if (!checksum || checksum !== current.snapshotChecksum) {
53	    return false;
54	  }
55	
56	  return next.snapshotRunId === current.snapshotRunId
57	    && next.snapshotSource === current.snapshotSource
58	    && next.messageScope?.complete === current.messageScope?.complete;
59	}
60	
61	function mergeByKey<T>(current: T[] | undefined, incoming: T[] | undefined, getKey: (item: T) => string | null | undefined) {
62	  const incomingItems = incoming ?? [];
63	  const seen = new Set<string>();
64	  const merged: T[] = [];
65	
66	  for (const item of incomingItems) {
67	    const key = getKey(item);
68	    if (key) {
69	      seen.add(key);
70	    }
71	    merged.push(item);
72	  }
73	
74	  for (const item of current ?? []) {
75	    const key = getKey(item);
76	    if (!key || seen.has(key)) {
77	      continue;
78	    }
79	    seen.add(key);
80	    merged.push(item);
81	  }
82	
83	  return merged;
84	}
85	
86	function runUpdatedTimestampMs(run: RunRecord) {
87	  const value = new Date(run.updatedAt || run.createdAt).getTime();
88	  return Number.isFinite(value) ? value : 0;
89	}
90	
91	function timestampMs(value: string | null | undefined) {
92	  if (!value) {
93	    return 0;
94	  }
95	
96	  const parsed = new Date(value).getTime();
97	  return Number.isFinite(parsed) ? parsed : 0;
98	}
99	
100	function mergeReadMarkersForVisibleRuns(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState) {
101	  const visibleRunIds = new Set((incoming.runs ?? []).map((run) => run.id));
102	  const merged: Record<string, string> = {};
103	
104	  for (const [runId, readAt] of Object.entries(current.readMarkers ?? {})) {
105	    if (visibleRunIds.has(runId)) {
106	      merged[runId] = readAt;
107	    }
108	  }
109	
110	  for (const [runId, readAt] of Object.entries(incoming.readMarkers ?? {})) {
111	    if (!visibleRunIds.has(runId)) {
112	      continue;
113	    }
114	    if (timestampMs(readAt) >= timestampMs(merged[runId])) {
115	      merged[runId] = readAt;
116	    }
117	  }
118	
119	  return merged;
120	}
121	
122	function mergeGoalSnapshots(
123	  current: EventStreamState,
124	  incoming: EventStreamState,
125	  serverAuthoritative: boolean,
126	) {
127	  const merged = { ...(current.goalsByRunId ?? {}) };
128	  const incomingGoals = incoming.goalsByRunId ?? {};
129	  for (const [runId, rawSnapshot] of Object.entries(incomingGoals)) {

133	      snapshot.revision === existing.revision

137	      merged[runId] = snapshot;

139	  }

143	    delete merged[scopedRunId];
144	  }
145	  const visibleRunIds = new Set((incoming.runs ?? []).map((run) => run.id));
146	  if (serverAuthoritative && incoming.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === true) {
147	    for (const runId of Object.keys(merged)) {
148	      if (!visibleRunIds.has(runId)) delete merged[runId];
149	    }
150	  }
151	  return merged;
152	}
153	
154	function mergeScopedCatalog(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState) {
155	  const scopedRunId = incoming.snapshotRunId?.trim() || null;
156	  const isSelectedRunScoped = Boolean(scopedRunId);
157	  const catalogIsComplete = incoming.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === true;
158	  const catalogIsPartial = incoming.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === false;
159	  if (catalogIsComplete || (!isSelectedRunScoped && !catalogIsPartial)) {
160	    return {
161	      ...incoming,
162	      readMarkers: mergeReadMarkersForVisibleRuns(current, incoming),
163	    };
164	  }
165	
166	  const selectedRunIsAbsent = Boolean(
167	    catalogIsPartial
168	    && scopedRunId
169	    && !(incoming.runs ?? []).some((run) => run.id === scopedRunId),
170	  );
171	  const removedPlanId = selectedRunIsAbsent
172	    ? current.runs.find((run) => run.id === scopedRunId)?.planId
173	    : null;
174	  const currentRuns = selectedRunIsAbsent
175	    ? current.runs.filter((run) => run.id !== scopedRunId)
176	    : current.runs;
177	  const currentPlans = removedPlanId
178	    ? current.plans.filter((plan) => plan.id !== removedPlanId)
179	    : current.plans;
180	  const currentWorkers = selectedRunIsAbsent
181	    ? current.workers.filter((worker) => worker.runId !== scopedRunId)
182	    : current.workers;
183	  const currentSessions = selectedRunIsAbsent
184	    ? current.sessions?.filter((session) => session.runId !== scopedRunId)
185	    : current.sessions;
186	  const mergedRuns = mergeByKey(currentRuns, incoming.runs, (run) => run.id);
187	  return {
188	    ...incoming,
189	    snapshotChecksum: catalogIsPartial ? current.snapshotChecksum : incoming.snapshotChecksum,
190	    runs: mergedRuns,
191	    plans: mergeByKey(currentPlans, incoming.plans, (plan) => plan.id),
192	    workers: mergeByKey(currentWorkers, incoming.workers, (worker) => worker.id),
193	    sessions: mergeByKey(currentSessions, incoming.sessions, (session) => session.runId),
194	    readMarkers: mergeReadMarkersForVisibleRuns(current, {
195	      ...incoming,
196	      runs: mergedRuns,
197	    }),
198	  };
199	}
200	
201	function mergeScopedRuns(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState, options: {
202	  serverAuthoritative: boolean;
203	}) {
204	  const catalogMergedIncoming = mergeScopedCatalog(current, incoming);
205	  if (options.serverAuthoritative) {
206	    return catalogMergedIncoming;
207	  }
208	
209	  const incomingRuns = catalogMergedIncoming.runs ?? [];
210	  if (incomingRuns.length === 0 || !current.runs?.length) {
211	    return catalogMergedIncoming;
212	  }
213	
214	  const currentRunsById = new Map(current.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
215	  let changed = false;
216	  const mergedRuns = incomingRuns.map((incomingRun) => {
217	    const currentRun = currentRunsById.get(incomingRun.id);
218	    if (!currentRun || runUpdatedTimestampMs(currentRun) <= runUpdatedTimestampMs(incomingRun)) {
219	      return incomingRun;
220	    }
221	
222	    changed = true;
223	    return {
224	      ...incomingRun,
225	      ...currentRun,
226	    };
227	  });
228	
229	  return changed ? { ...catalogMergedIncoming, runs: mergedRuns } : catalogMergedIncoming;
230	}
231	
232	/**
233	 * Supervisor-conversation messages still flow through the `messages`
234	 * table (worker-attributed messages moved to the unified worker
235	 * stream). Snapshots declare whether their message run scope is
236	 * complete; partial updates are additive and must not erase durable
237	 * conversation messages the client already knows about.
238	 */
239	function mergeScopedMessages(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState) {
240	  const incomingMessages = incoming.messages ?? [];
241	  const incomingMessageIds = new Set(incomingMessages.map((message) => message.id));
242	  const completeMessageRunIds = new Set(
243	    incoming.messageScope?.complete ? incoming.messageScope.runIds : [],
244	  );
245	  const liveRunIds = new Set((incoming.runs ?? []).map((run) => run.id));
246	  const retainedCurrentMessages = (current.messages ?? []).filter((message) => (
247	    liveRunIds.has(message.runId)
248	    && !incomingMessageIds.has(message.id)
249	    && !completeMessageRunIds.has(message.runId)
250	  ));
251	  const mergedMessages = sortMessages([
252	    ...retainedCurrentMessages,
253	    ...incomingMessages,
254	  ]);
255	
256	  if (
257	    mergedMessages.length === incomingMessages.length
258	    && mergedMessages.every((message, index) => message === incomingMessages[index])
259	  ) {
260	    return incoming;
261	  }
262	
263	  return {
264	    ...incoming,
265	    messages: mergedMessages,
266	  };
267	}
268	
269	/**
270	 * `bridgeMissing` agent snapshots are built without any live agent record,
271	 * so their `pendingPermissions`/`pendingElicitations` are hardcoded empty —
272	 * they mean "we could not reach the runtime", NOT "nothing is pending".
273	 * Letting them overwrite live values tore an open permission prompt or
274	 * elicitation form off the screen mid-answer whenever a degraded frame
275	 * landed between two live ones. A request may only be cleared by a frame
276	 * that actually talked to the runtime.
277	 */
278	function mergeDegradedAgentHumanInput(current: EventStreamState, incoming: EventStreamState) {
279	  const incomingAgents = incoming.agents ?? [];
280	  if (incomingAgents.length === 0 || !current.agents?.length) {
281	    return incoming;
282	  }
283	
284	  const currentAgentsByName = new Map(current.agents.map((agent) => [agent.name, agent]));
285	  let changed = false;
286	  const mergedAgents = incomingAgents.map((incomingAgent) => {
287	    if (!incomingAgent.bridgeMissing) {
288	      return incomingAgent;
289	    }
290	
291	    // Deliberately not gated on `currentAgent.bridgeMissing`: a run of
292	    // consecutive degraded frames must keep carrying the request forward, or
293	    // the second one re-opens the exact flicker the first one avoided. The
294	    // only things that may clear a pending request are a frame that reached
295	    // the runtime and the local optimistic update the answer path applies
296	    // (which goes through updateLocal, bypassing this merge).
297	    const currentAgent = currentAgentsByName.get(incomingAgent.name);
298	    if (!currentAgent) {
299	      return incomingAgent;
300	    }
301	
302	    const pendingPermissions = currentAgent.pendingPermissions ?? [];
303	    const pendingElicitations = currentAgent.pendingElicitations ?? [];
304	    if (pendingPermissions.length === 0 && pendingElicitations.length === 0) {
305	      return incomingAgent;
306	    }
307	
308	    changed = true;
309	    return {
310	      ...incomingAgent,
311	      pendingPermissions,
312	      pendingElicitations,
313	    };
314	  });
315	
316	  return changed ? { ...incoming, agents: mergedAgents } : incoming;
317	}
318	
319	function mergeScopedCachedState(current: EventStreamState, cached: EventStreamState): EventStreamState {
320	  // hydrateFromCacheScope swaps scopes (e.g. on session switch). The cached
321	  // payload for the newly-selected scope only contains data that was
322	  // observed while THAT scope was active — its messages/workers/agents/
323	  // queuedMessages/executionEvents are a strict subset of what we already
324	  // know across all runs. Replacing current collections with that subset
325	  // briefly empties any cross-run sidebar view (notably the Active tab,
326	  // whose filter is driven by messages+workers+agents+queuedMessages).
327	  //
328	  // Initial-mount cache hydration takes a different path (hydrateFromCaches
329	  // → snapshotCache.hydrateState) that already prefers current values when
330	  // present and falls back to cached when empty, so the only consumers of
331	  // this merge are scope transitions where current is server-authoritative.
332	  // Preserve current state and only adopt the scope-pointer metadata so
333	  // consumers gating on snapshotRunId still recognize the switch.
334	  return {
335	    ...current,
336	    snapshotRunId: cached.snapshotRunId ?? current.snapshotRunId,
337	    snapshotChecksum: cached.snapshotChecksum ?? current.snapshotChecksum,
338	    snapshotScope: cached.snapshotScope ?? current.snapshotScope,
339	    frontendErrors: [],
340	  };
341	}
342	
343	function authoritativeCatalogExcludesScope(state: EventStreamState, scope: string | null | undefined) {
344	  const scopedRunId = scope?.trim();
345	  if (!scopedRunId || state.snapshotSource !== "server" || state.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete !== true) {
346	    return false;
347	  }
348	
349	  return !(state.runs ?? []).some((run) => run.id === scopedRunId);
350	}
351	
352	/**
353	 * State manager for the global event snapshot stream. Worker
354	 * conversation content (bridge entries, user/supervisor inputs,
355	 * lifecycle markers) is NOT managed here — it lives in
356	 * `WorkerEntriesManager` and is fetched per-worker via
357	 * the typed worker-entry API. This manager owns runs, plans,
358	 * workers metadata, supervisor messages, planning artifacts, queued
359	 * messages, recovery state, and review records.
360	 */
361	export class EventStreamStateManager {
362	  private state: EventStreamState;
363	  private readonly listeners = new Set<EventStreamStateListener>();
364	  // A stop click is immediately terminal in the local UI. Keep that owned
365	  // result until the server confirms a terminal state; otherwise an in-flight
366	  // quota_waiting snapshot can reopen the recovery notice for one frame.
367	  private readonly pendingOptimisticStopRunIds = new Set<string>();
368	  private readonly snapshotCache: EventStreamSnapshotCacheManager;
369	  private snapshotCacheScope: string | null;
370	
371	  constructor(initialState: EventStreamState, options: {
372	    snapshotCache?: EventStreamSnapshotCacheManager;
373	    snapshotCacheScope?: string | null;
374	    deferCacheHydration?: boolean;
375	    initialSnapshotSource?: EventStreamSnapshotSource;
376	  } = {}) {
377	    this.snapshotCache = options.snapshotCache ?? new EventStreamSnapshotCacheManager();
378	    this.snapshotCacheScope = options.snapshotCacheScope?.trim() || null;
379	    const source = options.initialSnapshotSource;
380	    const initialWithSource = source ? { ...initialState, snapshotSource: source } : initialState;
381	    if (options.deferCacheHydration) {
382	      this.state = initialWithSource;
383	    } else {
384	      this.state = {
385	        ...this.snapshotCache.hydrateState(initialWithSource, this.snapshotCacheScope),
386	        snapshotSource: "cache",
387	      };
388	    }
389	  }
390	
391	  hydrateFromCaches() {
392	    if (authoritativeCatalogExcludesScope(this.state, this.snapshotCacheScope)) {
393	      return;
394	    }
395	    if (
396	      this.state.snapshotSource === "server"
397	      && (!this.snapshotCacheScope || this.state.snapshotRunId === this.snapshotCacheScope)
398	    ) {
399	      return;
400	    }
401	    const cached = this.snapshotCache.hydrateState(this.state, this.snapshotCacheScope);
402	    if (cached === this.state) {
403	      return;
404	    }
405	    this.state = { ...cached, snapshotSource: "cache" };
406	    for (const listener of this.listeners) {
407	      listener(this.state);
408	    }
409	  }
410	
411	  hydrateFromCacheScope(scope: string | null | undefined) {
412	    this.snapshotCacheScope = scope?.trim() || null;
413	    if (authoritativeCatalogExcludesScope(this.state, this.snapshotCacheScope)) {
414	      return false;
415	    }
416	    if (
417	      this.state.snapshotSource === "server"
418	      && this.snapshotCacheScope
419	      && this.state.snapshotRunId === this.snapshotCacheScope
420	    ) {
421	      return false;
422	    }
423	    const cached = this.snapshotCache.getCachedState(this.snapshotCacheScope);
424	    if (!cached || Object.is(cached, this.state)) {
425	      return false;
426	    }
427	
428	    this.state = {
429	      ...mergeScopedCachedState(this.state, cached),
430	      snapshotSource: this.state.snapshotSource === "server" ? "server" : "cache",
431	    };
432	    for (const listener of this.listeners) {
433	      listener(this.state);
434	    }
435	    return true;
436	  }
437	
438	  getSnapshot() {
439	    return this.state;
440	  }
441	
442	  subscribe(listener: EventStreamStateListener) {
443	    this.listeners.add(listener);
444	    return () => {
445	      this.listeners.delete(listener);
446	    };
447	  }
448	
449	  setSnapshotCacheScope(scope: string | null | undefined) {
450	    this.snapshotCacheScope = scope?.trim() || null;
451	  }
452	
453	  update(action: EventStreamStateAction, options: { snapshotSource?: EventStreamSnapshotSource } = {}) {
454	    const incoming = typeof action === "function" ? action(this.state) : action;
455	    const isServerUpdate = options.snapshotSource === "server";
456	    const snapshotSource = options.snapshotSource
457	      ?? incoming.snapshotSource
458	      ?? this.state.snapshotSource;
459	    let incomingWithRuns = mergeScopedRuns(this.state, incoming, {
460	      serverAuthoritative: snapshotSource === "server",
461	    });
462	    if (isServerUpdate && this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.size > 0) {
463	      const currentRunsById = new Map(this.state.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
464	      const incomingRunsById = new Map(incoming.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
465	      const protectedRunIds = new Set<string>();
466	
467	      for (const runId of this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds) {
468	        const serverRun = incomingRunsById.get(runId);
469	        // Terminal server truth acknowledges the stop and retires the overlay.
470	        if (isTerminalRunStatus(serverRun?.status)) {
471	          this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.delete(runId);
472	          continue;
473	        }
474	        if (!serverRun) {
475	          const catalogIsComplete = !incoming.snapshotRunId
476	            && incoming.snapshotScope?.catalog?.complete === true;
477	          if (catalogIsComplete) {
478	            this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.delete(runId);
479	          }
480	          continue;
481	        }
482	
483	        const currentRun = currentRunsById.get(runId);
484	        if (currentRun?.status === "cancelled") {
485	          protectedRunIds.add(runId);
486	        }
487	      }
488	
489	      if (protectedRunIds.size > 0) {
490	        incomingWithRuns = {
491	          ...incomingWithRuns,
492	          runs: incomingWithRuns.runs.map((run) => (
493	            protectedRunIds.has(run.id) ? currentRunsById.get(run.id) ?? run : run
494	          )),
495	        };
496	      }
497	    }
498	    const incomingWithAgents = mergeDegradedAgentHumanInput(this.state, incomingWithRuns);
499	    const nextState = {
500	      ...mergeScopedMessages(this.state, incomingWithAgents),
501	      goalsByRunId: mergeGoalSnapshots(this.state, incomingWithAgents, snapshotSource === "server"),
502	      snapshotSource,
503	    };
504	
505	    // `nextState` is always a fresh literal, so the old `Object.is` guard here
506	    // could never fire — every frame took a new identity and re-rendered the
507	    // whole shell, even for a byte-identical payload. Polls (the 5s snapshot
508	    // validation and the 15s SSE fallback) re-deliver unchanged snapshots
509	    // routinely, so this was a guaranteed floor of full-tree cascades while
510	    // idle. The server-computed checksum covers the whole payload, so an equal
511	    // checksum plus an unchanged merge result means genuinely nothing moved.
512	    if (isNoOpSnapshotUpdate(this.state, nextState)) {
513	      return this.state;
514	    }
515	
516	    this.state = nextState;
517	    this.snapshotCache.rememberState(nextState, this.snapshotCacheScope);
518	    this.listeners.forEach((listener) => listener(this.state));
519	    return this.state;
520	  }
521	
522	  /**
523	   * Apply an explicit local mutation without treating its intentionally
524	   * removed records as a partial server catalog that needs merging.
525	   */
526	  updateLocal(action: EventStreamStateAction) {
527	    const incoming = typeof action === "function" ? action(this.state) : action;
528	    const currentRunsById = new Map(this.state.runs.map((run) => [run.id, run]));
529	    const incomingRunIds = new Set(incoming.runs.map((run) => run.id));
530	    for (const run of incoming.runs) {
531	      const currentRun = currentRunsById.get(run.id);
532	      if (run.status === "cancelled" && currentRun && currentRun.status !== "cancelled") {
533	        this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.add(run.id);
534	      } else if (run.status !== "cancelled") {
535	        // Mutation error handlers restore their captured pre-stop state through
536	        // updateLocal, which explicitly releases the optimistic ownership.
537	        this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.delete(run.id);
538	      }
539	    }
540	    for (const runId of this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds) {
541	      if (!incomingRunIds.has(runId)) {
542	        this.pendingOptimisticStopRunIds.delete(runId);
543	      }
544	    }
545	    const nextState = {
546	      ...incoming,
547	      snapshotSource: this.state.snapshotSource,
548	    };
549	    if (Object.is(nextState, this.state)) {
550	      return this.state;
551	    }
552	    this.state = nextState;
553	    this.snapshotCache.rememberState(nextState, this.snapshotCacheScope);
554	    this.listeners.forEach((listener) => listener(this.state));
555	    return this.state;
556	  }
557	
558	  updateFromServer(action: EventStreamStateAction) {
559	    return this.update(action, { snapshotSource: "server" });
560	  }
561	
562	  applyGoalEvent(rawSnapshot: unknown, eventKey?: string | null) {
563	    const snapshot = parseGoalSnapshot(rawSnapshot);
564	    const current = this.state.goalsByRunId?.[snapshot.runId];
565	    if (current && current.revision >= snapshot.revision) return false;
566	    this.state = {
567	      ...this.state,
568	      goalsByRunId: {
569	        ...(this.state.goalsByRunId ?? {}),
570	        [snapshot.runId]: {
571	          ...snapshot,
572	          provenance: { ...snapshot.provenance, source: "server" },
573	        },
574	      },
575	    };
576	    this.snapshotCache.rememberState(this.state, this.snapshotCacheScope);
577	    this.listeners.forEach((listener) => listener(this.state));
578	    void eventKey;
579	    return true;
580	  }
581	}

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:28:21.697Z

I'm digging into how the server decides which runs to include in the snapshot catalog when a run is selected — checking the events route's ordering logic (sorted by last activity, creation time, then id) and cross-referencing with the persisted-snapshot logic to see if there's a cap limiting the run list.

My hypothesis is the snapshot caps runs by lastActivityAt plus always includes the selected one, but since the client re-sorts by lastActivityAt during merge, ordering shouldn't matter — unless lastActivityAt is actually missing from some payloads, which I want to verify by checking the persisted-snapshot logic more closely.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:28:41.073Z

The Projects tab ordering shouldn't be affected by clicking a session unless the selected run's lastActivityAt differs client-side. I'm wondering if opening a session triggers some side effect—like a read marker or checkpoint write—that could be reordering the list unexpectedly.

Actually the key insight: the `runs_activity_on_worker_turn_end` trigger bumps last_activity_at only when a worker's status transitions out of "working." If selecting a run causes its worker to reconcile or go idle, that bump would explain the jump to top. This also explains why a3c8783—still actively working—sinks below sessions that just finished a turn, since active runs don't get their timestamp bumped until they stop. The question is whether this is a recent regression, so I should check git history for when lastActivityAt was introduced.

596	/**
597	 * Live SSE frames only need the selected conversation's changing catalog
598	 * records. The client already received the complete catalog during snapshot
599	 * bootstrap and merges this explicitly partial slice by stable ids.
600	 *
601	 * Partial frames deliberately omit the complete-catalog checksum. Otherwise
602	 * a change to an unselected run could advance the client's checksum without
603	 * delivering that run and make the next complete validation return a false
604	 * `notModified` result.

606	function buildEventStreamPayload(
607	  payload: EventPayload,
608	  options: EventPayloadOptions,

611	  if (!selectedRunId) {
612	    return payload;

622	    plans: selectedPlanId

629	      : {},

631	      ...payload.snapshotScope,

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:29:12.895Z

Since SSE frames are scoped to the selected run, other runs' rows never get live updates on the client—their lastActivityAt stays frozen at the last full catalog snapshot. I need to check whether the periodic snapshot validation poll fetches with selectedRunId too, since that would explain why other runs never refresh.

Tracing further, the client's runs array for unselected runs only updates when a complete snapshot arrives, so I need to check the polling and notModified logic tied to snapshotChecksum comparisons—partial frames omit the checksum and mergeScopedCatalog preserves the old one. That should work if the checksum genuinely reflects lastActivityAt, but I suspect a possible bug: if the checksum is computed over only a subset of the payload for performance reasons, a lastActivityAt-only change on an unselected run might not alter the checksum at all, meaning the client would never learn about it and would keep showing frozen, stale ordering.

This would explain the odd behavior perfectly — an unselected run only updates once you select it, since the partial SSE frame then delivers its fresh lastActivityAt. That's exactly why clicking a session moves it to the top: clicking bba9b18 lets its partial frame deliver a fresh timestamp so it jumps up, while a3c8783 stays stuck below because its timestamp is frozen at the last complete snapshot until it too gets selected. I want to verify this by checking how the checksum is actually computed.

```text
import { createHash } from "node:crypto";

export function calculateEventPayloadChecksum(payload: Record<string, unknown>): string {
  const { snapshotChecksum: _snapshotChecksum, ...checksumPayload } = payload;
  return `sha256:${createHash("sha256")
    .update(JSON.stringify(checksumPayload))
    .digest("base64url")}`;
}

export function withEventPayloadChecksum<T extends Record<string, unknown>>(payload: T): T & { snapshotChecksum: string } {
  return {
    ...payload,
    snapshotChecksum: calculateEventPayloadChecksum(payload),
  };
}

60	type SnapshotPollResponse = EventStreamState | {

71	function withFallbackErrorContext(
72	  error: unknown,
73	  fallback: Pick<AppErrorDescriptor, "source" | "action">,
74	) {
75	  const normalized = normalizeAppError(error, fallback);
76	  return {
77	    ...normalized,
78	    source: normalized.source || fallback.source,
79	    action: normalized.action || fallback.action,
80	  };
81	}
82	
83	function isTransientConnectivityError(error: unknown) {
84	  if (!(error instanceof Error)) {
85	    return false;
86	  }
87	
88	  if (error.name === "AbortError" || error.name === "NetworkError") {
89	    return true;
90	  }
91	
92	  if (!(error instanceof TypeError)) {
93	    return false;
94	  }
95	
96	  const message = error.message.toLowerCase();
97	  return (
98	    message.includes("failed to fetch")
99	    || message.includes("network")
100	    || message.includes("load failed")
101	    || message.includes("offline")
102	  );
103	}
104	
105	function normalizeLastEventId(lastEventId: string | number | null | undefined) {
106	  const value = String(lastEventId ?? "").trim();
107	  return value || null;
108	}
109	
110	function compareEventIds(left: string, right: string) {
111	  if (parseEventStreamId(left) && parseEventStreamId(right)) {
112	    return compareEventStreamIds(left, right);
113	  }
114	  if (/^\d+$/.test(left) && /^\d+$/.test(right)) {
115	    const leftValue = BigInt(left);
116	    const rightValue = BigInt(right);
117	    return leftValue === rightValue ? 0 : leftValue > rightValue ? 1 : -1;
118	  }
119	  return left === right ? 0 : null;
120	}
121	
122	export class LiveEventCursorManager {
123	  private lastEventId: string | null = null;
124	  private readonly storage: Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem"> | null;
125	  private readonly storageKey: string | null;
126	
127	  constructor(
128	    initialLastEventId?: string | number | null,
129	    options: {
130	      scopeKey?: string | null;
131	      storage?: Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem"> | null;
132	    } = {},
133	  ) {
134	    this.storage = options.storage ?? null;
135	    const scopeKey = options.scopeKey?.trim() ?? "";
136	    this.storageKey = scopeKey
137	      ? `omniharness:event-cursor:${scopeKey}`
138	      : null;
139	    if (this.storage && this.storageKey) {
140	      this.advance(this.storage.getItem(this.storageKey));
141	    }
142	    this.advance(initialLastEventId);
143	  }
144	
145	  getCurrent() {
146	    return this.lastEventId;
147	  }
148	
149	  advance(lastEventId: string | number | null | undefined) {
150	    const normalized = normalizeLastEventId(lastEventId);
151	    if (!normalized) return false;
152	    if (this.lastEventId) {
153	      const ordering = compareEventIds(normalized, this.lastEventId);
154	      if (ordering !== null && ordering <= 0) {
155	        return false;
156	      }
157	    }
158	    this.lastEventId = normalized;
159	    if (this.storage && this.storageKey) {
160	      safeSetBrowserStorageItem(this.storage, this.storageKey, normalized);
161	    }
162	    return true;
163	  }
164	
165	  clear() {
166	    this.lastEventId = null;
167	    if (this.storage && this.storageKey) {
168	      this.storage.removeItem(this.storageKey);
169	    }
170	  }
171	}
172	
173	function isNotModifiedSnapshot(value: SnapshotPollResponse): value is Extract<SnapshotPollResponse, { notModified: true }> {
174	  return typeof value === "object" && value !== null && "notModified" in value && value.notModified === true;
175	}
