```text
177	export class LiveEventConnectionManager {
178	  private readonly selectedRunId?: string | null;
179	  private readonly events: Pick<RuntimeAPIs["events"], "snapshot" | "open">;
180	  private readonly getSnapshotChecksum?: () => string | null | undefined;
181	  private readonly workerEntries: Pick<typeof workerEntriesManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onStreamResync" | "onWakeUp">;
182	  private readonly planManager: Pick<AcpPlanManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onStreamResync" | "onWakeUp">;
183	  private readonly sidebarWorkerActivity: Pick<SidebarWorkerActivityManager, "onKnownSeqs" | "onWakeUp">;
184	  private readonly applyUpdate: (state: EventStreamState) => void;
185	  private readonly applyGoalEvent?: (snapshot: GoalSnapshot, eventKey: string | null) => boolean;
186	  private readonly reportError: (error: AppErrorDescriptor) => void;
187	  private readonly onStreamResync?: () => void;
188	  private readonly fallbackIntervalMs: number;
189	  private readonly fallbackCooldownMs: number;
190	  private readonly snapshotValidationIntervalMs: number | null;
191	  private eventSource: RuntimeSubscription | null = null;
192	  private fallbackTimer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
193	  private snapshotValidationTimer: ReturnType<typeof setInterval> | null = null;
194	  private active = false;
195	  private pollingSnapshot = false;
196	  private snapshotPollPromise: Promise<boolean> | null = null;
197	  private reconnectingAfterResync = false;
198	  private lastSnapshotPollAt = 0;
199	  private readonly cursor: LiveEventCursorManager;
200	  private connectionGeneration = 0;
201	
202	  constructor(options: LiveEventConnectionManagerOptions) {
203	    this.selectedRunId = options.selectedRunId;
204	    this.cursor = options.cursor ?? new LiveEventCursorManager(options.initialLastEventId);
205	    this.cursor.advance(options.initialLastEventId);
206	    this.events = options.events ?? createLegacyLiveEventAPIs({
207	      EventSourceConstructor: options.EventSourceConstructor,
208	      requestJson: options.requestJson,
209	      requestSnapshot: options.requestSnapshot,
210	    });
211	    this.getSnapshotChecksum = options.getSnapshotChecksum;
212	    this.workerEntries = options.workerEntries ?? workerEntriesManager;
213	    this.planManager = options.planManager ?? acpPlanManager;
214	    this.sidebarWorkerActivity = options.sidebarWorkerActivity ?? sidebarWorkerActivityManager;
215	    this.applyUpdate = options.applyUpdate;
216	    this.applyGoalEvent = options.applyGoalEvent;
217	    this.reportError = options.reportError;
218	    this.onStreamResync = options.onStreamResync;
219	    this.fallbackIntervalMs = options.fallbackIntervalMs ?? SNAPSHOT_FALLBACK_INTERVAL_MS;
220	    this.fallbackCooldownMs = options.fallbackCooldownMs ?? SNAPSHOT_FALLBACK_COOLDOWN_MS;
221	    this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs = options.snapshotValidationIntervalMs === undefined
222	      ? SNAPSHOT_VALIDATION_INTERVAL_MS
223	      : options.snapshotValidationIntervalMs;
224	  }
225	
226	  start() {
227	    if (this.active) {
228	      return;
229	    }
230	
231	    this.active = true;
232	    this.openEventSource();
233	
234	    this.startSnapshotValidation();
235	    void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
236	  }
237	
238	  private openEventSource() {
239	    this.eventSource = this.events.open({
240	      snapshot: false,
241	      runId: this.selectedRunId,
242	      lastEventId: this.cursor.getCurrent(),
243	    }, {
244	      onOpen: () => {
245	        this.stopFallbackPolling();
246	      },
247	      onEvent: (event) => {
248	        const streamEvent = event as {
249	          kind?: string;
250	          payload?: unknown;
251	          lastEventId?: string | null;
252	        };
253	        if (streamEvent.kind !== "update" && streamEvent.lastEventId && !this.cursor.advance(streamEvent.lastEventId)) {
254	          return;
255	        }
256	        if (streamEvent.kind === "update") {
257	          this.handleUpdateEvent(streamEvent);
258	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "update_error") {
259	          this.handleUpdateErrorEvent(streamEvent.payload);
260	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "worker.entry_appended") {
261	          this.handleWorkerEntryAppended(streamEvent.payload);
262	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "worker.plan_updated" || streamEvent.kind === "worker.plan_boundary_started") {
263	          this.handlePlanWakeUp(streamEvent.payload);
264	        } else if (streamEvent.kind?.startsWith("goal.")) {
265	          this.handleGoalEvent(streamEvent.payload);
266	        } else if (streamEvent.kind === "stream.resync_required") {
267	          void this.handleStreamResyncRequired();
268	        }
269	      },
270	      onError: () => {
271	        this.startFallbackPolling();
272	        void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
273	      },
274	    });
275	  }
276	
277	  stop() {
278	    this.active = false;
279	    this.connectionGeneration++;
280	    this.stopFallbackPolling();
281	    this.stopSnapshotValidation();
282	    this.eventSource?.close();
283	    this.eventSource = null;
284	  }
285	
286	  private handleUpdateEvent(event: { payload?: unknown; lastEventId?: string | null }) {
287	    try {
288	      if (event.lastEventId && !this.cursor.advance(event.lastEventId)) {
289	        return;
290	      }
291	      const data = event.payload as EventStreamState;
292	      if (!data || typeof data !== "object") {
293	        throw new TypeError("Invalid live update.");
294	      }
295	      this.stopFallbackPolling();
296	      this.applyUpdate(data);
297	      this.workerEntries.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
298	      this.planManager.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
299	      this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
300	    } catch {
301	      this.reportError({
302	        message: "The frontend received a malformed live update payload.",
303	        source: "Events",
304	        action: "Process live updates",
305	        suggestion: "Inspect the events route response payload and server logs, then refresh the page after fixing the malformed data.",
306	      });
307	    }
308	  }
309	
310	  private handleWorkerEntryAppended(payload: unknown) {
311	    try {
312	      const data = payload as { workerId?: unknown; seq?: unknown; runId?: unknown };
313	      const workerId = typeof data.workerId === "string" ? data.workerId : null;
314	      const seq = typeof data.seq === "number" ? data.seq : null;
315	      const runId = typeof data.runId === "string" ? data.runId : null;
316	      if (!workerId || seq == null) {
317	        return;
318	      }
319	      this.workerEntries.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq });
320	      this.planManager.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq, runId });
321	      this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onWakeUp({ workerId, seq, runId });
322	    } catch {
323	      // Malformed frames are ignored — the next valid frame (or the
324	      // periodic snapshot poll) will re-sync any missed entries.
325	    }
326	  }
327	
328	  private handlePlanWakeUp(payload: unknown) {
329	    const data = payload as { workerId?: unknown; runId?: unknown; seq?: unknown };
330	    if (typeof data.workerId !== "string" || typeof data.seq !== "number") return;
331	    this.planManager.onWakeUp({
332	      workerId: data.workerId,
333	      runId: typeof data.runId === "string" ? data.runId : null,
334	      seq: data.seq,
335	    });
336	  }
337	
338	  private handleGoalEvent(payload: unknown) {
339	    if (!this.applyGoalEvent || !payload || typeof payload !== "object") return;
340	    const event = payload as { snapshot?: unknown; eventKey?: unknown };
341	    if (!event.snapshot) return;
342	    try {
343	      this.applyGoalEvent(
344	        event.snapshot as GoalSnapshot,
345	        typeof event.eventKey === "string" ? event.eventKey : null,
346	      );
347	    } catch (error) {
348	      this.reportError(withFallbackErrorContext(error, {
349	        source: "Goals",
350	        action: "Process goal update",
351	      }));
352	      void this.handleStreamResyncRequired();
353	    }
354	  }
355	
356	  private handleUpdateErrorEvent(payload: unknown) {
357	    try {
358	      this.reportError(withFallbackErrorContext(payload, {
359	        source: "Events",
360	        action: "Stream live updates",
361	      }));
362	    } catch {
363	      this.reportError({
364	        message: "The live event stream reported a malformed error payload.",
365	        source: "Events",
366	        action: "Stream live updates",
367	      });
368	    }
369	
370	    this.startFallbackPolling();
371	    void this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
372	  }
373	
374	  private async handleStreamResyncRequired() {
375	    this.workerEntries.onStreamResync();
376	    this.planManager.onStreamResync();
377	    this.onStreamResync?.();
378	    if (!this.active || this.reconnectingAfterResync) {
379	      return;
380	    }
381	
382	    this.reconnectingAfterResync = true;
383	    this.eventSource?.close();
384	    this.eventSource = null;
385	    this.cursor.clear();
386	    try {
387	      const snapshotLoaded = await this.pollSnapshot({ force: true });
388	      if (!this.active || !snapshotLoaded) {
389	        this.startFallbackPolling();
390	        return;
391	      }
392	
393	      this.openEventSource();
394	    } finally {
395	      this.reconnectingAfterResync = false;
396	    }
397	  }
398	
399	  private startFallbackPolling() {
400	    if (this.fallbackTimer) {
401	      return;
402	    }
403	
404	    this.fallbackTimer = setInterval(() => {
405	      void this.pollSnapshot();
406	    }, this.fallbackIntervalMs);
407	  }
408	
409	  private stopFallbackPolling() {
410	    if (!this.fallbackTimer) {
411	      return;
412	    }
413	
414	    clearInterval(this.fallbackTimer);
415	    this.fallbackTimer = null;
416	  }
417	
418	  private startSnapshotValidation() {
419	    if (
420	      this.snapshotValidationTimer
421	      || this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs == null
422	      || this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs <= 0
423	    ) {
424	      return;
425	    }
426	
427	    this.snapshotValidationTimer = setInterval(() => {
428	      void this.pollSnapshot();
429	    }, this.snapshotValidationIntervalMs);
430	  }
431	
432	  private stopSnapshotValidation() {
433	    if (!this.snapshotValidationTimer) {
434	      return;
435	    }
436	
437	    clearInterval(this.snapshotValidationTimer);
438	    this.snapshotValidationTimer = null;
439	  }
440	
441	  private async pollSnapshot(options: { force?: boolean } = {}): Promise<boolean> {
442	    if (this.pollingSnapshot) {
443	      return this.snapshotPollPromise ?? Promise.resolve(false);
444	    }
445	
446	    const now = Date.now();
447	    if (
448	      !this.active
449	      || (!options.force && now - this.lastSnapshotPollAt < this.fallbackCooldownMs)
450	    ) {
451	      return false;
452	    }
453	
454	    this.lastSnapshotPollAt = now;
455	    this.pollingSnapshot = true;
456	    this.snapshotPollPromise = this.runSnapshotPoll();
457	    try {
458	      return await this.snapshotPollPromise;
459	    } finally {
460	      this.pollingSnapshot = false;
461	      this.snapshotPollPromise = null;
462	    }
463	  }
464	
465	  private async runSnapshotPoll(): Promise<boolean> {
466	    const gen = this.connectionGeneration;
467	    try {
468	      const result = await this.events.snapshot({
469	        runId: this.selectedRunId,
470	        persisted: true,
471	        checksum: this.getSnapshotChecksum?.() ?? null,
472	      }) as SnapshotPollResult;
473	      if (!this.active || gen !== this.connectionGeneration) {
474	        return false;
475	      }
476	      this.setLastEventId(result.lastEventId);
477	      const data = result.data;
478	      if (isNotModifiedSnapshot(data)) {
479	        this.workerEntries.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
480	        this.planManager.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
481	        this.sidebarWorkerActivity.onKnownSeqs(data.workerEntrySeqs);
482	        return true;
483	      }
```

I found it: the SNAPSHOT_VALIDATION_INTERVAL_MS was bumped from 5s to 60s in a recent commit fixing event stream efficiency, which matches the timing of when this started happening. That means ordering data for non-selected runs is now stale for up to a full minute, and since SSE partial frames only update the selected run, unselected run ordering only refreshes on that slower interval.

I need to trace whether the snapshot endpoint returns the full catalog or the partial payload when a runId is specified, and check how mergeScopedCatalog handles the checksum across partial SSE frames versus complete polls — there might be a subtle bug where the checksum gets set incorrectly after a partial frame arrives, causing the next complete snapshot to be skipped as a no-op when it shouldn't be.

Actually, thinking about ordering logic itself: if a run is only reordered when lastActivityAt changes, and that only updates on user messages or turn completion, then a run spinning for a long time legitimately stays where it is — that seems like intended behavior, not a bug. I want to check the actual commits around 09-05 that touched run payload or ordering logic to see what changed.

```text
+/**

+    plans: selectedPlanId

+    workers: payload.workers.filter((worker) => worker.runId === selectedRunId),
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:30:05.048Z

I've found the regression: two combined changes cause it. SSE frames now only update the selected run's catalog record, freezing every other run's lastActivityAt, and the full-catalog refresh interval dropped from 5s to 60s—so unselected runs go stale while clicking a run pulls its fresh data immediately, making it jump to the top.

But I'm now questioning whether spinning runs should sort by lastActivityAt at all, since that only updates on user messages or turn ends—technically correct semantics, but it explains why users perceive sudden multi-place jumps. I need to trace whether the 60s poll's snapshot handler actually returns the complete catalog payload, and whether checksum comparison correctly triggers a full resend when server-side run rows change, since a stale client checksum against a changed server payload should force reconciliation—unless the merge logic for scoped catalog updates has its own bug.

Now I realize switching the selected run tears down and restarts the SSE connection with a forced full snapshot poll, meaning clicking any session actually refreshes every run's lastActivityAt at once—which explains why a3c8783 jumped from 1st to 4th: its top position was an artifact of receiving its own partial frame updates while selected, and the forced refresh exposed the real order. I'm trying to determine whether that reordering on click is actually the correct behavior or a side effect worth fixing.

Read markers alone don't touch lastActivityAt, so that's probably not it. Maybe selecting a run triggers worker reconciliation or a status transition (like working→idle) that fires the trigger updating lastActivityAt, or the SSE reconnect itself causes a runtime refresh that changes worker status. I should actually check the code path for what happens on run selection — like useRunSelectionEffects — instead of continuing to speculate.

Actually, there's a simpler explanation for why the clicked run jumps to the top that doesn't need any server-side writes: it's about ordering in the merge logic, where incoming runs get placed first and the group list gets re-sorted by lastActivityAt — but that should make array position irrelevant unless there are ties in lastActivityAt that fall back to createdAt or id ordering. So I need to check whether the partial SSE frame actually carries a fresh lastActivityAt value for that run.

Now I'm wondering if unselected runs lose their lastActivityAt somewhere in the non-authoritative merge path when SSE updates get applied — I need to trace how applyUpdate is wired into the home lifecycle and bootstrap code, and whether the frame is treated as server-authoritative with an incomplete catalog. If so, the merge should still work fine, so I should instead check what happens server-side when a run gets selected, since some reconciliation or goal-control logic tied to selection might be the real trigger here.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:30:29.012Z

I notice workers are only loaded for the selected run, which affects the Active tab's spinner state but not the Projects tab ordering. Stepping back, I need to pin down what's actually broken in the recency computation the user is asking about.

Checking whether a complete snapshot poll still returns the full run catalog when a run is selected — tracing through the snapshot request options and confirming that `readPersistedEventRecords` loads all runs unconditionally, so the complete snapshot does include everything and recency should converge within 60 seconds.

So after that window the ordering should be correct, but the real complaint is about the reordering behavior in between polls and the jumps triggered by clicks. Checking whether SSE partial frames apply with server-authoritative merging — confirming that `mergeScopedCatalog` merges the incoming selected run into current state, keeping the selected run first while preserving the snapshot checksum, then looking at how read markers get merged for visible runs in that partial update path.

The worker merge for the partial path just combines current and incoming workers for the selected run, which looks fine. I want to rule out one more possible source of the jumping: whether the initial SSE snapshot event could ever hand the client stale-but-authoritative data that overwrites fresher local state, and whether session-switch caching (via the snapshot cache manager) could reintroduce old cached state for a run scope — but tracing through `mergeScopedCachedState`, it only adopts scope pointers from cache and preserves current data, so that path looks safe too.

Actually I think I found the real explanation: since `sendUpdateIfChanged` diffs the serialized partial-frame payload, once a run is selected, changes to *other* runs stop producing a different partial payload, so no SSE frame is sent for those changes — that's by design, and the 60-second checksum poll is the backstop, capping staleness at a minute. But the more likely culprit for the "nonsensical" ordering the user sees is that ordering is based on `lastActivityAt`, which only updates on message send or turn completion — so a session that's been actively running for 20 minutes without finishing a turn will rank *below* one where the user merely typed something 5 minutes ago, even though the running session feels "most recent" to the user.

I want to check whether this ordering behavior changed at some point — I should look at what `compareConversationRunsDesc` (or the equivalent sort logic) looked like before commit 369ab11, to see if the server used to sort by `updatedAt` instead.

```text
+function timestampMs(value: string | null | undefined) {
+  if (!value) return 0;
+  const parsed = new Date(value).getTime();
+  return Number.isFinite(parsed) ? parsed : 0;
+}
+
+/**
+ * Order sessions by conversation activity, newest first.
+ *
+ * `lastActivityAt` is written server-side on the last user message and the last
+ * finished agent turn (see the `runs_activity_*` triggers in
+ * `src/server/db/index.ts`). Sorting here rather than relying on the snapshot's
+ * `ORDER BY` matters because `EventStreamStateManager` merges runs by id and
+ * appends unseen ones to the end — an optimistically created run would
+ * otherwise sink to the bottom of its project until the next full snapshot.
+ */
+function compareConversationRunsDesc(
+  a: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
+  b: { lastActivityAt?: string | null; createdAt: string; id: string },
+) {
+  const activityDiff =
+    Math.max(timestampMs(b.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(b.createdAt))
+    - Math.max(timestampMs(a.lastActivityAt), timestampMs(a.createdAt));
+  if (activityDiff !== 0) return activityDiff;
+  const createdDiff = timestampMs(b.createdAt) - timestampMs(a.createdAt);
+  if (createdDiff !== 0) return createdDiff;
+  return a.id < b.id ? -1 : a.id > b.id ? 1 : 0;
+}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:31:05.459Z

I want to verify empirically rather than just reason abstractly: with a run selected, the payload only includes that single run, so other runs' lastActivityAt values simply stop being sent over SSE. Combined with the refresh interval going from 5s to 60s, the catalog now updates twelve times less often — I should check if there's a second contributing factor too.

I traced through the checksum logic and confirmed it's computed over the complete payload including selected-run extras, so switching runs correctly triggers a full payload refresh. Now I want to check whether the SSE connection itself restarts when selectedRunId changes, which would force a full snapshot poll.

Actually, maybe the click-to-top behavior has a simpler explanation: selecting a run might reset the connection manager and trigger a full re-fetch, meaning the order shown right after clicking is genuinely fresh data rather than something odd. But that only explains things if the clicked session truly has the newest lastActivityAt — and two different sessions can't both be newest each time they're clicked, so something on selection must actually be bumping lastActivityAt. I want to check whether marking a run as read on selection touches the runs table somehow, separate from the read markers table.

The sort comparison uses the max of lastActivityAt and createdAt, so that's not it. What catches my attention is worker reconciliation on selection — the events route seems to run a reconciliation step for the selected run's workers on every request, and if that flips worker statuses out of "working," it could trigger an update that bumps lastActivityAt, pushing the selected run to the top. That feels like the real culprit worth confirming.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01EcHLywFSENwjVJpJ8wpEBA · 2026-09-06T19:31:05.511Z

```text
import { asc, eq } from "drizzle-orm";
import path from "path";
import { db } from "@/server/db";
import { runs, workers } from "@/server/db/schema";
import { emitNamedEvent } from "@/server/events/named-events";
import { openRecoveryIncident, markRecoveryIncidentNeedsUser } from "@/server/runs/recovery-incidents";
import { setRunNeedsRecovery } from "@/server/runs/recovery-actions";
import { isTerminalRunStatus, normalizeRunStatus } from "@/server/runs/status";
import { resumeMissingDirectWorker } from "@/server/conversations/send-message";
import { notifyEventStreamSubscribers } from "@/server/events/live-updates";
import { readWorkerSessionMetadata } from "@/server/workers/session-metadata";

const STARTING_WORKER_RELOAD_GRACE_MS = 30_000;
const ACTIVE_WORKER_RELOAD_GRACE_MS = 15_000;
const LOST_WORKER_REASON = "Worker was marked active but is not present in the bridge runtime.";

function normalizedWorkerStatus(worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect) {
  return normalizeRunStatus(worker.status).split(":")[0]?.trim() ?? "";
}

function isPastGrace(worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect, nowMs: number) {
  const status = normalizedWorkerStatus(worker);
  const graceMs = status === "starting"
    ? STARTING_WORKER_RELOAD_GRACE_MS
    : ACTIVE_WORKER_RELOAD_GRACE_MS;
  return nowMs - worker.updatedAt.getTime() >= graceMs;
}

function isPathInside(root: string, candidate: string) {
  const relative = path.relative(root, candidate);
  return relative === "" || (!relative.startsWith("..") && !path.isAbsolute(relative));
}

function workerMatchesRunProject(run: typeof runs.$inferSelect, worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect) {
  if (!run.projectPath?.trim()) {
    return true;
  }
  const projectPath = path.resolve(run.projectPath);
  const workerCwd = path.resolve(worker.cwd);
  return isPathInside(projectPath, workerCwd);
}

function isRunEligible(status: string | null | undefined) {
  const normalized = normalizeRunStatus(status);
  return Boolean(normalized)
    && !isTerminalRunStatus(normalized)
    && normalized !== "awaiting_user"
    && normalized !== "needs_recovery"
    && normalized !== "recovering"
    && normalized !== "quota_waiting";
}

function isStaleStartingWorker(worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect, nowMs: number) {
  if (normalizedWorkerStatus(worker) !== "starting") {
    return false;
  }
  if (worker.bridgeSessionId?.trim()) {
    return false;
  }
  return isPastGrace(worker, nowMs);
}

// Workers that were actively producing output when the bridge died:
// `status="working"` in the DB but absent from the bridge's live agent
// list. Without this, the FE shows "running" in the sidebar and no
// "Thinking…" indicator forever, because the DB row never advances and
// the bridge has no agent to report state for. We auto-resume via the
// same spawn-with-saved-session path that the regular send-message
// flow uses.
function isOrphanedActiveWorker(
  worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect,
  bridgeAgentNames: ReadonlySet<string>,
  nowMs: number,
): boolean {
  const status = normalizedWorkerStatus(worker);
  if (status !== "starting" && status !== "working" && status !== "stuck" && status !== "recovering") {
    return false;
  }
  // The bridge knows about it — nothing for us to do.
  if (bridgeAgentNames.has(worker.id)) {
    return false;
  }
  return isPastGrace(worker, nowMs);
}

async function markWorkerLost(worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect) {
  if (normalizedWorkerStatus(worker) === "lost") {
    return;
  }
  await db.update(workers).set({
    status: "lost",
    currentText: "",
    updatedAt: new Date(),
  }).where(eq(workers.id, worker.id));
  emitNamedEvent({
    kind: "worker.status",
    runId: worker.runId,
    workerId: worker.id,
    prev: worker.status,
    next: "lost",
  });
}

async function markStaleWorkerNeedsUser(args: {
  run: typeof runs.$inferSelect;
  worker: typeof workers.$inferSelect;
  source?: string;
  reason: string;
  autoRecoveryError?: string | null;
}) {
  await markWorkerLost(args.worker);
  await setRunNeedsRecovery({
    runId: args.run.id,
    reason: args.reason,
  });
  const incident = await openRecoveryIncident({
    runId: args.run.id,
    workerId: args.worker.id,
    queuedMessageId: null,
    kind: "worker_lost",
    lastError: args.reason,
    details: {
      source: args.source ?? "persisted-reload-bootstrap",
      recoveryState: "needs_recovery",
      recommendedAction: "manual_resume",
      reason: args.reason,
      ...(args.autoRecoveryError ? { autoRecoveryError: args.autoRecoveryError } : {}),
    },
  });
  await markRecoveryIncidentNeedsUser({
    incidentId: incident.id,
    runId: args.run.id,
    workerId: args.worker.id,
    reason: args.reason,
    details: {
      recoveryState: "needs_recovery",
      recommendedAction: "manual_resume",
      reason: args.reason,
    },
  });

uuid: 449d5205-e846-4ed0-9243-8871a7a09935
parent: b1a91ddd-7f3b-4a40-a7db-a176d4735fd6
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:31:13.501Z

I'm checking whether the main loop scopes to selectedRunId, and tracing how resumeMissingDirectWorker spawning a worker can flip its status from working to lost — since markWorkerLost sets status to "lost" with a fresh updatedAt, the trigger fires when the prior status was "working" and updates run.last_activity_at to now. So selecting a run triggers an events request scoped to selectedRunId, which feeds into reconcilePersistedReloadZombies for that same run.

If that run has an orphaned worker, flipping it from working to lost bumps lastActivityAt to now and sends the run to the top of its project — which is exactly the "clicking session X moves it to the top" behavior, and explains why the spinning session a3c8783 shows a stale lastActivityAt. Now I want to check how the reconciler scopes its work.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01TDGMYP1kU2KDSaJaZC4vmm · 2026-09-06T19:31:13.571Z

```text
  });

  return {
    action: "needs_user" as const,
    runId: args.run.id,
    workerId: args.worker.id,
    incidentId: incident.id,
  };
}

export async function reconcilePersistedReloadZombies(args: {
  selectedRunId?: string | null;
  source?: string;
  nowMs?: number;
  // Set of worker ids the bridge has live agent records for. When
  // provided, we additionally detect workers whose DB status is
  // "working"/"stuck" but who aren't in the bridge — usually because
  // the bridge crashed/restarted while they were mid-turn. When
  // undefined, we skip this check (we don't know what the bridge
  // sees yet).
  bridgeAgentNames?: ReadonlySet<string>;
}) {
  const runId = args.selectedRunId?.trim();
  if (!runId) {
    return { action: "none" as const };
  }

  const run = await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, runId)).get();
  if (!run || !isRunEligible(run.status)) {
    return { action: "none" as const };
  }

  const nowMs = args.nowMs ?? Date.now();
  const runWorkers = await db
    .select()
    .from(workers)
    .where(eq(workers.runId, run.id))
    .orderBy(asc(workers.createdAt), asc(workers.id));

  // Prefer the orphaned-working case when the caller has told us what
  // the bridge sees: a "working" row with no live agent is the more
  // urgent failure (UI hangs forever) than a "starting" row past its
  // grace window.
  const orphanedActiveWorker = args.bridgeAgentNames && run.mode !== "implementation"
    ? runWorkers.find((worker) => isOrphanedActiveWorker(worker, args.bridgeAgentNames!, nowMs))
    : undefined;
  const staleWorker = orphanedActiveWorker
    ?? runWorkers.find((worker) => isStaleStartingWorker(worker, nowMs));

  if (!staleWorker) {
    return { action: "none" as const };
  }
  if (!workerMatchesRunProject(run, staleWorker)) {
    return { action: "none" as const };
  }

  // The worker is alive in DB but its bridge session is gone. Instead of
  // surfacing a "needs recovery" incident with a manual Resume button —
  // which is the exact button the user keeps having to press after every
  // crash/restart — try to auto-respawn via the same primitive on-send
  // recovery uses. The runtime gets a fresh agent record (with the saved
  // session resumed when the agent CLI supports it), and the user just
  // sees the conversation come back to life.
  const hasSavedSession = Boolean(
    staleWorker.bridgeSessionId?.trim()
    || await readWorkerSessionMetadata(staleWorker.runId, staleWorker.id),
  );
  if (!hasSavedSession) {
    return markStaleWorkerNeedsUser({
      run,
      worker: staleWorker,
      source: args.source,
      reason: `${LOST_WORKER_REASON} (missing persisted ACP session metadata)`,
    });
  }

  try {
    await resumeMissingDirectWorker(run, staleWorker);
    notifyEventStreamSubscribers();
    return {
      action: "recovered" as const,
      runId: run.id,
      workerId: staleWorker.id,
    };
  } catch (error) {
    // Auto-recovery failed — fall through to the original needs_user path so
    // the user has at least a button to fall back to. Most likely cause: the
    // agent CLI is unavailable (PATH issue) or its session resume is rejected
    // AND a fresh spawn fails. Both are genuinely user-actionable.
    const fallbackReason = error instanceof Error && error.message
      ? `${LOST_WORKER_REASON} (auto-recovery failed: ${error.message})`
      : LOST_WORKER_REASON;

    return markStaleWorkerNeedsUser({
      run,
      worker: staleWorker,
      source: args.source,
      reason: fallbackReason,
      autoRecoveryError: error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error),
    });
  }
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:31:25.124Z

I confirm that `reconcilePersistedReloadZombies` only acts on the selected run, flipping worker status which triggers activity tracking and updates `last_activity_at`—but this only fires for stale/orphaned workers past a grace period, so it wouldn't hit on every click unless both sessions happen to be in that state. I'm also considering `resumeMissingDirectWorker`, which likely transitions worker status from starting to working.

Now a stronger theory forms: the runtime sync loop only syncs workers for the currently *selected* run, per `events.ts` around line 164 where workers are loaded and snapshots built only for the selected run. If worker status reconciliation with the bridge only happens for the selected run's workers, then while run A is selected, run B's worker could finish its turn without any DB write occurring—until B gets selected, at which point the write fires the trigger and sets `last_activity_at` to now, causing B to jump to the top. This would perfectly explain the user's issue: recency is effectively tied to selection time rather than actual turn-end time. I want to trace where worker status gets synced from the bridge or runtime to confirm this.

## Claude tool result: toolu_01VxBH22XwvPBZSQuRXtRCrV · 2026-09-06T19:31:38.047Z

```text
let bridgeSyncInFlight: Promise<void> | null = null;

/**
 * Watchdog-driven reconciliation between the bridge runtime and persisted
 * worker/run state. Turn completions are normally persisted by the awaiting
 * send-message call, or by the SSE-driven sync that runs while a client is
 * connected — but both die with their in-flight request (server restart,
 * dropped ACP roundtrip, phone backgrounded the tab). A worker then sits at
 * status='working' forever while the bridge agent finished its turn long
 * ago, and the stuck-worker reaper keeps cancelling and re-delivering the
 * same prompt every timeout window. This sweep closes the gap from the 15s
 * watchdog without requiring any connected client.
 */
export function syncConversationSessionsFromBridge(): Promise<void> {
  if (bridgeSyncInFlight) {
    return bridgeSyncInFlight;
  }

  bridgeSyncInFlight = (async () => {
    let rawAgents: unknown[];
    try {
      rawAgents = await listAgents({ retryIndefinitely: false });
    } catch {
      // Bridge down or unreachable — there is no live state to reconcile
      // against; the next sweep will retry.
      return;
    }
    await syncConversationSessions(rawAgents);
  })().finally(() => {
    bridgeSyncInFlight = null;
  });
  return bridgeSyncInFlight;
}

type SyncConversationSessionsOptions = {
  selectedRunId?: string | null;
  refreshPlanningArtifacts?: boolean;
};

let liveSyncQueue: Promise<void> = Promise.resolve();

export function __resetSyncConversationSessionsQueueForTests() {
  liveSyncQueue = Promise.resolve();
}

export async function syncConversationSessions(rawAgents: unknown[], options: SyncConversationSessionsOptions = {}) {
  const runSync = () => syncConversationSessionsUnlocked(rawAgents, options);
  const nextSync = liveSyncQueue.then(runSync, runSync);
  liveSyncQueue = nextSync.catch(() => undefined);
  return nextSync;
}

async function syncConversationSessionsUnlocked(rawAgents: unknown[], options: SyncConversationSessionsOptions = {}) {
  const agents = rawAgents.map((agent) => normalizeAgentRecord(agent));
  const selectedRunId = options.selectedRunId?.trim() || null;
  const refreshPlanningArtifacts = options.refreshPlanningArtifacts !== false;
  const allRuns = selectedRunId
    ? await db.select().from(runs).where(eq(runs.id, selectedRunId))
    : await db.select().from(runs);
  const allWorkers = selectedRunId
    ? await db.select().from(workers).where(eq(workers.runId, selectedRunId))
    : await db.select().from(workers);
```

```text
import os from "node:os";
import path from "node:path";

function isPathInside(candidate: string, parent: string) {
  const relative = path.relative(parent, candidate);
  return relative !== "" && relative !== ".." && !relative.startsWith(`..${path.sep}`) && !path.isAbsolute(relative);
}

function isTestProcess() {
  return process.env.VITEST === "true"
    || process.env.VITEST_WORKER_ID !== undefined
    || process.env.TEST_WORKER_INDEX !== undefined;
}

function assertSafeTestRoot(root: string) {
  if (!isTestProcess()) {
    return;
  }

  const resolvedRoot = path.resolve(root);
  const resolvedCwd = path.resolve(process.cwd());
  const temporaryParents = [os.tmpdir(), "/tmp", "/var/tmp"].map((entry) => path.resolve(entry));
  const isTemporary = temporaryParents.some((parent) => isPathInside(resolvedRoot, parent));

  if (resolvedRoot === resolvedCwd || !isTemporary) {
    throw new Error(
      `[database-safety] Refusing to use non-temporary app data root in a test process: ${resolvedRoot}`,
    );
  }
}

export function getAppRoot() {
  const configuredRoot = process.env.OMNIHARNESS_ROOT?.trim();
  const root = configuredRoot ? path.resolve(configuredRoot) : process.cwd();
  assertSafeTestRoot(root);
  return root;
}

export function getAppDataPath(...segments: string[]) {
  return path.join(getAppRoot(), ...segments);
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:32:26.680Z

The database order actually makes sense: a3c8783 at 21:23, bba9b18 at 20:28, then 90629 at 18:47, so the server-side truth is correct and it's the client display that's out of date. The user saw 90629 ranked above a3c8783, meaning the client must have been holding a stale last_activity_at value for a3c8783 that predated 18:47.

That's odd though, since the 60-second validation poll should catch and refresh this. I'm wondering if the poll is incorrectly returning notModified, or if there's a discrepancy between how the SSE stream updates the checksum versus how the snapshot route computes it server-side. I want to check whether the snapshot handler's notModified comparison uses the complete payload's checksum or something tied to the partial stream, since that mismatch could explain why updates get dropped silently.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:32:57.844Z

I'm tracing whether the 60s poll actually refreshes runs, or whether SSE partial frames could overwrite them first—checking if applyUpdate treats the server as authoritative and merges the scoped catalog by key when only a partial update comes through.

Then I reconsider the user's actual report: clicking session bba9b18 moved it to the top even though a3c8783 has a later last_activity_at (21:23 vs 20:28). That would only make sense if the click itself bumped bba9b18's timestamp to now—meaning the user probably clicked around 20:28, and a3c8783's 21:23 bump happened afterward, not before.

So my hypothesis is that selecting a run writes last_activity_at = now, triggered by the scoped events sync for the selected run persisting a worker status transition that the global 15-second sweep missed, which then sets last_activity_at via the turn-end trigger. I still need to figure out why the global sweep wouldn't have caught that transition first.

Let me actually verify this against the data — checking whether the workers table shows updated_at for a3c8783's workers matching 21:23.

```text
const runSupervisorWatchdogSweep = createSupervisorWatchdogSweepRunner(syncRunningSupervision);

export async function ensureSupervisorRuntimeStarted() {
  if (!startupPromise) {
    startupPromise = syncRunningSupervision().then(() => {
      if (!watchdogInterval) {
        watchdogInterval = setInterval(() => {
          void runSupervisorWatchdogSweep();
        }, WATCHDOG_INTERVAL_MS);
      }
    }).catch((error) => {
      startupPromise = null;
      throw error;
    });
  }

  await startupPromise;
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:34:16.992Z

I'm tracing how persisting worker output entries and detecting a completed turn flips the worker status to idle, which triggers an update of run.last_activity_at. But since the global sweep also scans every run and agent, it seems like it would trigger the same update unless the bridge restricts scope somehow.

Comparing the global sync path and the scoped events path, both actually fetch all agents from the bridge, so there's no real distinction there — the turn-end write timing is just whatever it is, within a 15-second window. That makes me reconsider whether the mechanism I'm hunting for even exists, or if what the user's observing is just client-side staleness. Looking at real timestamps, the correlation between clicking a conversation and its turn ending at that exact moment could be coincidence, or there's another mechanism entirely — like selecting a run in the UI actually triggering delivery of queued messages or resuming something server-side.

I'm now considering whether the selected run gets continuously refreshed via SSE while unselected runs stay frozen between 60-second polls, which would produce exactly the described symptom: the selected run's lastActivityAt staying perpetually current since it's updated on every turn-end.

I trace the caching layer further and confirm the payload is keyed by version and rebuilt fresh on each change, meaning client staleness should be capped at 60 seconds, so I can't yet fully explain why one run appears out of order relative to a timestamp from hours earlier without some other stale data path.

I'm now considering the caching layer more closely — how the initial state gets hydrated from a persisted snapshot before the first poll runs. If that cached snapshot carries a checksum that happens to match what the server returns, the client would treat it as "not modified" and keep the stale runs, even though the cached data actually came from an earlier session entirely.

```text
import type { AgentSnapshot, EventStreamState } from "./types";
import type { GoalSnapshot } from "@/shared/goal-plan";

const DEFAULT_STORAGE_KEY = "omni-event-stream-snapshot-cache:v1";
const DEFAULT_MAX_SNAPSHOTS = 8;
const DEFAULT_MAX_SERIALIZED_BYTES = 4_000_000;
const GLOBAL_SCOPE_KEY = "__global__";
// The event stream pushes a fresh snapshot for every agent output change,
// which on a chatty run is many frames per second. Serializing and writing
// the whole multi-megabyte envelope on each one blocked the main thread long
// enough to drop keystrokes in the composer — the worse the agent's output
// rate, the worse the typing lag, on any session. Reads are served from the
// in-memory envelope; the storage write is coalesced onto a trailing timer.
const DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS = 1_000;

type SnapshotStorage = Pick<Storage, "getItem" | "setItem" | "removeItem">;

interface CachedSnapshot {
  updatedAt: number;
  state: EventStreamState;
}

interface SnapshotEnvelope {
  version: 1;
  snapshots: Record<string, CachedSnapshot>;
}

interface EventStreamSnapshotCacheManagerOptions {
  storage?: SnapshotStorage | null;
  storageKey?: string;
  maxSnapshots?: number;
  maxSerializedBytes?: number;
  now?: () => number;
  flushIntervalMs?: number;
}

function scopeKey(scope: string | null | undefined) {
  return scope?.trim() || GLOBAL_SCOPE_KEY;
}

function emptyEnvelope(): SnapshotEnvelope {
  return {
    version: 1,
    snapshots: {},
  };
}

function compactAgent(agent: AgentSnapshot): AgentSnapshot {
  return {
    ...agent,
    outputEntries: agent.outputEntries?.map((entry) => ({
      ...entry,
      raw: undefined,
    })),
  };
}

function compactStateForCache(state: EventStreamState): EventStreamState {
  return {
    ...state,
    agents: (state.agents ?? []).map(compactAgent),
    frontendErrors: [],
  };
}

function preferInitialArray<T>(
  initial: T[] | undefined,
  cached: T[] | undefined,
  options: { serverAuthoritative: boolean },
) {
  if (options.serverAuthoritative && initial) {
    return initial;
  }

  return initial && initial.length > 0 ? initial : cached ?? [];
}

function isEventStreamState(value: unknown): value is EventStreamState {
  if (typeof value !== "object" || value === null) {
    return false;
  }

  const record = value as Partial<EventStreamState>;
  return Array.isArray(record.runs) && Array.isArray(record.messages);
}

function hydrateGoals(initialState: EventStreamState, cachedState: EventStreamState) {
  if (initialState.snapshotSource === "server" && initialState.goalsByRunId) {
    return initialState.goalsByRunId;
  }
  const goals: Record<string, GoalSnapshot> = Object.fromEntries(
    Object.entries(cachedState.goalsByRunId ?? {}).map(([runId, goal]) => [runId, {
      ...goal,
      provenance: { ...goal.provenance, source: "cache" as const },
    }]),
  );
  for (const [runId, goal] of Object.entries(initialState.goalsByRunId ?? {})) {
    if (!goals[runId] || goal.revision >= goals[runId].revision) goals[runId] = goal;
  }
  return goals;
}

export class EventStreamSnapshotCacheManager {
  private readonly storage: SnapshotStorage | null;
  private readonly storageKey: string;
  private readonly maxSnapshots: number;
  private readonly maxSerializedBytes: number;
  private readonly now: () => number;
  private readonly flushIntervalMs: number;
  private envelope: SnapshotEnvelope | null = null;
  private flushTimer: ReturnType<typeof setTimeout> | null = null;

  constructor(options: EventStreamSnapshotCacheManagerOptions = {}) {
    // Server/SSE state is authoritative. Persistent snapshot bodies can
    // consume the entire origin quota, so browser storage is opt-in for
    // isolated tests or explicitly bounded consumers only.
    this.storage = options.storage ?? null;
    this.storageKey = options.storageKey ?? DEFAULT_STORAGE_KEY;
    this.maxSnapshots = options.maxSnapshots ?? DEFAULT_MAX_SNAPSHOTS;
    this.maxSerializedBytes = options.maxSerializedBytes ?? DEFAULT_MAX_SERIALIZED_BYTES;
    this.now = options.now ?? Date.now;
    this.flushIntervalMs = options.flushIntervalMs ?? DEFAULT_FLUSH_INTERVAL_MS;
  }

  hydrateState(initialState: EventStreamState, scope: string | null | undefined = null) {
    const cachedState = this.getCachedState(scope);
    if (!cachedState) {
      return initialState;
    }
    const arrayMergeOptions = {
      serverAuthoritative: initialState.snapshotSource === "server",
    };

    return {
      ...cachedState,
      ...initialState,
      messages: preferInitialArray(initialState.messages, cachedState.messages, arrayMergeOptions),
      plans: preferInitialArray(initialState.plans, cachedState.plans, arrayMergeOptions),
      runs: preferInitialArray(initialState.runs, cachedState.runs, arrayMergeOptions),
      accounts: preferInitialArray(initialState.accounts, cachedState.accounts, arrayMergeOptions),
      agents: preferInitialArray(initialState.agents, cachedState.agents, arrayMergeOptions),
      workers: preferInitialArray(initialState.workers, cachedState.workers, arrayMergeOptions),
      planItems: preferInitialArray(initialState.planItems, cachedState.planItems, arrayMergeOptions),
      clarifications: preferInitialArray(initialState.clarifications, cachedState.clarifications, arrayMergeOptions),
      executionEvents: preferInitialArray(initialState.executionEvents, cachedState.executionEvents, arrayMergeOptions),
      supervisorInterventions: preferInitialArray(initialState.supervisorInterventions, cachedState.supervisorInterventions, arrayMergeOptions),
      queuedMessages: preferInitialArray(initialState.queuedMessages, cachedState.queuedMessages, arrayMergeOptions),
      recoveryIncidents: preferInitialArray(initialState.recoveryIncidents, cachedState.recoveryIncidents, arrayMergeOptions),
      goalsByRunId: hydrateGoals(initialState, cachedState),
      frontendErrors: [],
    };
  }

  getCachedState(scope: string | null | undefined = null): EventStreamState | null {
    const snapshot = this.readEnvelope().snapshots[scopeKey(scope)];
    if (!snapshot || !isEventStreamState(snapshot.state)) {
      return null;
    }

    return {
      ...snapshot.state,
      frontendErrors: [],
    };
  }

  /**
   * O(1) on the hot path: the envelope is mutated in memory and the
   * expensive part (compaction, serialization, the synchronous storage
   * write) is deferred to a single trailing flush. Reads go through the same
   * in-memory envelope, so a `rememberState` is observable immediately.
   */
  rememberState(state: EventStreamState, scope: string | null | undefined = null) {
    if (!this.storage) {
      return;
    }

    const currentEnvelope = this.readEnvelope();
    this.envelope = this.pruneEnvelope({
      ...currentEnvelope,
      snapshots: {
        ...currentEnvelope.snapshots,
        [scopeKey(scope)]: {
          updatedAt: this.now(),
          state,
        },
      },
    });

    this.scheduleFlush();
  }

  /** Write the pending envelope to storage now, cancelling any scheduled flush. */
  flush() {
    if (this.flushTimer !== null) {
      clearTimeout(this.flushTimer);
      this.flushTimer = null;
    }

    const envelope = this.envelope;
    if (!this.storage || !envelope) {
      return;
    }

    const serialized = JSON.stringify({
      version: 1,
      snapshots: Object.fromEntries(
        Object.entries(envelope.snapshots).map(([key, snapshot]) => [key, {
          updatedAt: snapshot.updatedAt,
          state: compactStateForCache(snapshot.state),
        }]),
      ),
    } satisfies SnapshotEnvelope);
    if (serialized.length > this.maxSerializedBytes) {
      return;
    }

    try {
      this.storage.setItem(this.storageKey, serialized);
    } catch {
      // localStorage can be unavailable or full. The live stream remains the source of truth.
    }
  }

  private scheduleFlush() {
    if (this.flushTimer !== null) {
      return;
    }

    if (this.flushIntervalMs <= 0) {
      this.flush();
      return;
    }

    this.flushTimer = setTimeout(() => {
      this.flushTimer = null;
      this.flush();
    }, this.flushIntervalMs);
    // Never hold the process open for a preview cache.
    (this.flushTimer as { unref?: () => void }).unref?.();
  }

  private readEnvelope(): SnapshotEnvelope {
    if (!this.storage) {
      return emptyEnvelope();
    }

    if (this.envelope) {
      return this.envelope;
    }

    try {
      const raw = this.storage.getItem(this.storageKey);
      if (!raw) {
        this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
        return this.envelope;
      }

      const parsed = JSON.parse(raw) as Partial<SnapshotEnvelope>;
      if (parsed.version !== 1 || typeof parsed.snapshots !== "object" || parsed.snapshots === null) {
        this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
        return this.envelope;
      }

      this.envelope = {
        version: 1,
        snapshots: parsed.snapshots as Record<string, CachedSnapshot>,
      };
      return this.envelope;
    } catch {
      try {
        this.storage.removeItem(this.storageKey);
      } catch {
        // Ignore cleanup failures.
      }
      this.envelope = emptyEnvelope();
      return this.envelope;
    }
  }

  private pruneEnvelope(envelope: SnapshotEnvelope): SnapshotEnvelope {
    const snapshots = Object.fromEntries(
      Object.entries(envelope.snapshots)
        .sort((left, right) => {
          const updatedDelta = right[1].updatedAt - left[1].updatedAt;
          return updatedDelta !== 0 ? updatedDelta : left[0].localeCompare(right[0]);
        })
        .slice(0, this.maxSnapshots),
    );

    return {
      version: 1,
      snapshots,
    };
  }
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:34:44.142Z

When the client polls with persisted mode, it goes through buildPersistedEventPayload to get the complete catalog reflecting SQLite state. But I notice this path skips buildSharedRuntimeEnrichedEventPayload, so the agents data lacks bridge enrichment—meaning its checksum would differ structurally from the SSE frames' payload.

This creates a mismatch: if the client's checksum comes from an SSE runtime-enriched frame rather than a persisted poll, it'll never match the persisted endpoint's checksum, forcing a full refresh every time—which is actually harmless, just extra data. So every 60 seconds the client should get the full catalog from SQLite; the stale display shouldn't persist for hours unless the poll itself isn't running, perhaps because startSnapshotValidation's pollSnapshot guard against concurrent polls means a hung poll could block all future polling indefinitely.

If the fetch inside that poll never times out and never settles, `pollingSnapshot` stays true forever and no further polls fire—leaving the client frozen on old catalog data while only the selected run stays fresh via SSE partial frames. That would exactly match the reported symptom of stale data on unselected runs but live updates on the selected one, though I want to double check whether the client actually re-renders when the underlying state does change before committing to this theory.

Looking again at how partial merges combine current and incoming runs, the merge logic correctly prioritizes fresh data for the selected run while keeping stale entries for others, which confirms that path behaves as expected. What I still need to verify is whether the persisted payload returned by the poll marks the catalog as complete, since that determines whether the merge takes the full-refresh path or the partial-merge path.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:35:40.885Z

So with a run selected, the LiveEventConnectionManager handles subscriptions rather than the snapshot polling. I'm consolidating my analysis now, reconsidering whether the user's report is consistent with a 60-second poll model, and reconstructing the timeline—their observations likely happened shortly before the session was created around 21:27, with their message arriving around 21:32.

Looking at the specific timestamps the user mentioned, bba9b18's last activity was 20:28, yet clicking it moved it to the top above a3c8783 (21:23) and above 90629 as well. If the click was accurate and triggered a fresh poll, this ranking makes no sense since a3c8783 is clearly more recent — and if the click itself bumped bba9b18's timestamp, its displayed value should read close to the click time, not 20:28. Since neither the "accurate data" nor "click bumps timestamp" explanations fit, I'm concluding the client must have been holding a stale value for a3c8783 that predates even 18:47, meaning its last_act was effectively frozen near its creation time around 17:49 despite later user messages.

This lines up with the theory that the client falls back to `createdAt` whenever `lastActivityAt` is missing, matching how `compareConversationRunsDesc` handles null values. So it seems the selected run gets a real, current lastActivityAt while all other runs are only ordered by their createdAt timestamps — which would explain why the just-clicked run jumps to the top while the rest reshuffle based on creation order rather than actual recency.

So lastActivityAt is missing from run records except for the selected run - I need to figure out where it's being stripped for non-selected runs. Checking stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields, it only nulls out plannerArtifactsJson, not lastActivityAt, so that's not the source. I'm now suspecting the initial bootstrap payload has a separate implementation in persisted-snapshot.ts that handles this differently.

Now confirming that persisted-snapshot.ts does serialize lastActivityAt, and checking whether the runtime events route does the same. The events route seems to pass runs through with raw Date objects that JSON.stringify properly converts to ISO strings, so that path looks fine too. This means the field must be getting dropped somewhere on the client side, possibly in a filtering function within useHomeLifecycle that projects specific run fields and may be excluding lastActivityAt in the process.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:36:16.555Z

I'm checking whether lastActivityAt gets dropped for non-selected runs in the SSE payload, tracing through how stripUnusedRunSnapshotFields processes the raw drizzle rows before JSON serialization converts Date objects to ISO strings—so far both selected and non-selected runs should carry the field consistently, but I'm now wondering if the database select query itself is the culprit.

Actually, that's not the issue either since last_activity_at is included in the drizzle schema. So the asymmetry must come from timing—maybe the client's catalog view is stale from an earlier page load, frozen at some point before certain runs existed. I'm working through different freeze-time hypotheses to see which one would produce a3c8783 appearing below 90629 in the ordering, since for that to happen a3c8783's client-side timestamp would need to be earlier than 18:47 while 90629's stays fresher.

None of the timing windows I've tried give a clean explanation, which makes the null-lastActivityAt-with-createdAt-fallback theory much more attractive—it explains the ordering and the "clicked run jumps to top" behavior without relying on a lucky coincidence of when the catalog happened to freeze. So I'm now leaning toward: the selected run actually has a lastActivityAt value while the others fall back to null, and I want to trace where in the codebase that null value could originate, possibly in the event bridge/runner forwarding code that's untracked and new.

Rather than keep reasoning about it abstractly, let me just check if the app is actually running right now so I can hit the events endpoint directly and look at the real JSON—there's a runner lock file present, so it's worth verifying.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:36:49.677Z

Auth would require a session cookie, so that path isn't worth pursuing. Instead I'm comparing the wire representation of selected versus non-selected run records, both passing through the same field-stripping function, and they look identical in shape—both carry lastActivityAt. That makes me wonder about the merge logic for scoped catalogs when the catalog is partial and the client has no prior complete catalog to merge against.

Reconsidering the sort comparator that falls back between lastActivityAt and createdAt, I'm now suspecting a type mismatch: if lastActivityAt somehow arrives as a raw epoch number instead of an ISO string, parsing it as a Date would produce a date near 1970, tiny enough that the comparator falls back to createdAt—which would exactly explain the fallback behavior I'm chasing. That raises the question of whether a raw SQLite integer could leak through instead of a proper Date object during serialization.

Now I'm second-guessing that though, since drizzle timestamps should already come through as Date objects. I'm wondering if the issue is somewhere else entirely, like a proxy or bridge that re-serializes the run payload, or whether the discrepancy in units (seconds vs milliseconds) between last_activity_at and created_at could differ across bootstrap versus live paths. I'm also considering whether the real issue isn't a field mismatch at all, but which runs actually populate the client's runs array—possibly non-selected runs retain stale data from the initial bootstrap snapshot rather than getting updated by live partial-frame merges.

Now I'm digging into whether the persisted-snapshot serializer that adds lastActivityAt is only applied during bootstrap, while the runtime events route just emits raw Date objects without going through that same serializer—though both should still end up as ISO strings once JSON-encoded, so that path seems fine too. I'm wondering now if the snapshot response for that events route skips manual JSON.stringify and instead goes through something like Response.json, which could handle serialization differently.

Actually, let me reconsider the whole premise—maybe the freeze isn't about which field is captured but about the polling and checksum logic itself. If the client's persisted snapshot poll at T0 gets checksum C0 with full run data, and SSE only sends partial updates afterward while the checksum stays at C0, then on the next 60-second poll the server would only send a fresh payload if something in the entire payload changed relative to C0—otherwise it returns notModified, which could explain frozen values for runs that didn't change server-side.

Let me check whether last_activity_at is actually null for many non-archived runs, though that would only explain those specific runs, not the pattern I'm seeing. I'm also wondering if there's a compaction function for runs analogous to compactWorkerRecord for workers, applied only in the SSE path within buildEventPayload — I should check the events-route test file, which has been modified in the working tree, for expectations about lastActivityAt in payloads.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:37:31.716Z

The regression narrows down to commit 6591057, which introduced partial SSE frames and 60s validation—that timing lines up exactly with the "a few days ago" report. I want to double-check this against the staleness model once more, this time accounting for when the frozen catalog snapshot actually gets taken relative to run selection.

Actually, I realize `mergeScopedCatalog` only merges partial frames onto the prior state, and switching runs forces a full refresh via `pollSnapshot({force:true})`—so between switches only the active run's data updates on that 60s cycle. That should still tick regularly though, unless the snapshot promise itself is stalling; I want to check whether `events.snapshot` in web.ts has a timeout or abort condition that could explain it never firing again.

```text
function runParameter(runId: string | null | undefined, prefix: "?" | "&") {
  const value = runId?.trim();
  return value ? `${prefix}runId=${encodeURIComponent(value)}` : "";
}

export function buildEventStreamUrl(
  selectedRunId?: string | null,
  lastEventId?: string | number | null,
) {
  const runParam = runParameter(selectedRunId, "?");
  const cursor = String(lastEventId ?? "").trim();
  return `/api/events${runParam}${cursor
    ? `${runParam ? "&" : "?"}cursor=${encodeURIComponent(cursor)}`
    : ""}`;
}

export function buildPersistedSnapshotUrl(
  selectedRunId?: string | null,
  checksum?: string | null,
) {
  const checksumParam = checksum?.trim()
    ? `&checksum=${encodeURIComponent(checksum.trim())}`
    : "";
  return `/api/events?snapshot=1&persisted=1${runParameter(selectedRunId, "&")}${checksumParam}`;
}

export function createLegacyLiveEventAPIs(options: {
  EventSourceConstructor?: LegacyEventSourceConstructor;
  requestJson?: LegacyJsonRequester;
  requestSnapshot?: LegacySnapshotRequester;
  fetchImpl?: typeof fetch;
}): Pick<RuntimeAPIs["events"], "snapshot" | "open"> {
  const EventSourceConstructor = options.EventSourceConstructor
    ?? EventSource as unknown as LegacyEventSourceConstructor;

    async snapshot(input): Promise<RuntimeSnapshotResult> {
      const url = buildPersistedSnapshotUrl(input.runId, input.checksum);
      const fallback = {
        source: "Events",
        action: "Load live state snapshot",
      };
      if (options.requestSnapshot) {
        const result = await options.requestSnapshot(url, undefined, fallback);
        return {
          data: result.data,
          lastEventId: result.lastEventId ?? null,
        };
      }
      if (options.requestJson) {
        return {
          data: await options.requestJson(url, undefined, fallback),
          lastEventId: null,
        };
      }
      const fetchImpl = options.fetchImpl ?? fetch;
      const response = await fetchImpl(url, undefined);
      if (!response.ok) {
        throw new AppRequestError(await parseErrorResponse(response, fallback));
      }
      return {
        data: await response.json(),
        lastEventId: response.headers.get("x-omni-last-event-id"),
      };
    },
    open(input, handlers: EventStreamHandlers): RuntimeSubscription {
      const source = new EventSourceConstructor(
        buildEventStreamUrl(input.runId, input.lastEventId),
      );
      const eventTypes = [
        "update",
        "update_error",
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:38:16.074Z

I'm also noting production may use a different injected events API, and there's no fetch timeout, meaning a hung request could freeze polling entirely — a plausible but unprovable amplifier. Overall, I'm now confident the core mechanism is that run recency stops updating for every non-selected session once a session gets selected, leaving the Projects list showing one live record mixed with stale ones.

This resyncs only on the 60-second validation poll or a selection change, and since sorting relies on `lastActivityAt`, the list reshuffles in bursts that look random — with whichever run you click guaranteed to look freshest. I've verified this against the DB ordering, the SSE payload behavior introduced in the September 5th commit, and the validation interval change in that same commit, which together explain why things "used to work fine" before.

Now I'm thinking through the fix: one option is to include a lightweight recency slice in partial SSE frames — sending just id, status, timestamps, and title for every run — which is cheap and restores live ordering without giving up the payload-size savings, while keeping the partial frame marked incomplete.

But I realize `mergeByKey` replaces whole records, so sending trimmed rows for non-selected runs would wipe out fields like planId and projectPath needed for grouping. So either I need to send full stripped run rows for every run in each frame, or add a separate lightweight `runActivity` array that gets merged client-side alongside the full records — and since the heavy JSON fields are already stripped elsewhere, the remaining run catalog size is probably modest enough to send in full without undoing the original optimization.

Actually a better path might be to avoid stuffing this into every frame at all — I could emit it as a named event only when a run's last_activity_at actually changes, carrying just `{runId, lastActivityAt, status}`, and let the client patch that single record. That's much cheaper than resending a catalog slice on every frame, though it means adding emission hooks at the TS call sites where activity updates happen rather than relying on the SQL trigger alone.

Since the server already computes the full catalog payload on each tick before trimming it down, the cleanest fix is to only include the run catalog in the frame when it's actually changed since the last frame sent on that connection — that keeps things nearly free in steady state while staying instantly correct.

I need to track a per-connection `lastRunsPayload`, comparing serialized run data against what was last sent, and only attach the full runs array when it differs — being careful to keep the existing partial-frame checksum semantics untouched since that's a separate concern. Writing connection state tracking...

But I realize there's a wrinkle when the partial catalog carries all runs: `mergeScopedCatalog` still treats it as incomplete, so it merges by key with incoming winning, which is fine since deletions get handled by the periodic complete poll — though I need to double check how the "selected run is absent" pruning logic interacts with a partial catalog flag.

This does seem like the fix the user actually wants; they reported broken recency computation and this is a direct-control harness authorizing me to act. I should also verify whether the trimming affects run status/spinner behavior, since workers are also scoped to the selected run now, which could explain the spinner bug they mentioned.

I'm checking whether workers being scoped only to the selected run is a pre-existing issue or introduced by this commit, since that affects the Active tab's "isWorking" status for other runs beyond just the selected one.

Let me stay focused on the actual reported bug: fixing run-catalog recency in the SSE frames. I'll go look at the SSE handler code around lines 980-1000 to implement the fix there.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:38:53.812Z

I'm designing the approach to only send the full run catalog when it actually changes, tracking the last serialized version per connection so the SSE handler can compare and avoid redundant payloads.

Thinking through the edge case where the catalog reverts to a previous state and the outer payload check causes an early return without updating the tracked catalog value — this is fine since a stale flag would just cause the next frame to unnecessarily resend the catalog, which isn't harmful, just slightly redundant.

I also realize marking the full catalog as complete would break plan lookups needed for grouping conversations by run, since replacing plans wholesale would drop data for other runs — so complete must stay false to preserve that scoped merging behavior.

But then I'm worried resending the full catalog on any run-catalog change gets too chatty, since updated_at bumps constantly during an active turn across many call sites. So instead I'm refining to only include run rows that actually changed since the last frame, filtering by comparing each run's serialized form against what was previously sent.

I'll keep a per-connection map tracking the last serialized state of each run by id, and diff against that on every payload to find which runs actually changed. The first frame naturally sends everything as changed, giving a full catalog once per connection, which is expected and fine.

I'm feeding the changed run ids into the stream payload builder as additional runs, then only updating the "last serialized" snapshot at the point we actually write a frame, not on the identical-payload early return. I need to be careful though: if changedRunIds is non-empty but the resulting stream payload still matches the last emitted payload, the update logic could get inconsistent.

Good, nothing lost there. Now I'm thinking about newly-created runs whose plans aren't in the frame since plans get filtered to the selected run's plan — the client's grouping logic falls back to projectPath so it's mostly fine, but I should also include plans referenced by any run in the frame, which is cheap to add. Let me write that change, and reconsider whether the 60s validation poll is still needed now that the delta fix keeps live ordering correct.

Now I'm looking at the current payload-building function that filters runs and plans down to just the selected run — I need to modify it so the plans array also includes any plan referenced by runs in the frame, not just the selected plan.

Finishing building the payload...

The behavior matches the original when there's no delta, just filtered to include changed runs and their associated plans. Now I need to find where `lastUpdatePayload` is declared so I can place the new state variable appropriately.

```json
  "old_string": "/**\n * Live SSE frames only need the selected conversation's changing catalog\n * records. The client already received the complete catalog during snapshot\n * bootstrap and merges this explicitly partial slice by stable ids.\n *\n * Partial frames deliberately omit the complete-catalog checksum. Otherwise\n * a change to an unselected run could advance the client's checksum without\n * delivering that run and make the next complete validation return a false\n * `notModified` result.\n */\nfunction buildEventStreamPayload(\n  payload: EventPayload,\n  options: EventPayloadOptions,\n) {\n  const selectedRunId = options.selectedRunId?.trim();\n  if (!selectedRunId) {\n    return payload;\n  }\n\n  const selectedRun = payload.runs.find((run) => run.id === selectedRunId);\n  const selectedPlanId = selectedRun?.planId;\n  const { snapshotChecksum: _snapshotChecksum, ...streamPayload } = payload;\n  return {\n    ...streamPayload,\n    snapshotRunId: selectedRunId,\n    runs: selectedRun ? [selectedRun] : [],\n    plans: selectedPlanId\n      ? payload.plans.filter((plan) => plan.id === selectedPlanId)\n      : [],",
  "new_string": "/**\n * Live SSE frames only need the selected conversation's changing catalog\n * records, plus any *other* run row that changed since the last frame on this\n * connection. The client already received the complete catalog during snapshot\n * bootstrap and merges this explicitly partial slice by stable ids.\n *\n * The changed-run delta is not optional politeness: the sidebar sorts every\n * project by `runs.lastActivityAt`, so a frame that carries only the selected\n * run freezes every other row's sort key until the next complete validation\n * poll a minute later. The list then reshuffles in bursts, and whichever\n * conversation the user opens is the only one with a current key — so it always\n * appears newest. Sending the rows that actually changed keeps ordering live\n * without putting the whole catalog on every frame.\n *\n * Partial frames deliberately omit the complete-catalog checksum. Otherwise\n * a change to an unselected run could advance the client's checksum without\n * delivering that run and make the next complete validation return a false\n * `notModified` result.\n */\nfunction buildEventStreamPayload(\n  payload: EventPayload,\n  options: EventPayloadOptions,\n  catalogDelta: { changedRunIds?: ReadonlySet<string> } = {},\n) {\n  const selectedRunId = options.selectedRunId?.trim();\n  if (!selectedRunId) {\n    return payload;\n  }\n\n  const changedRunIds = catalogDelta.changedRunIds;\n  const runs = payload.runs.filter((run) => (\n    run.id === selectedRunId || Boolean(changedRunIds?.has(run.id))\n  ));\n  // Grouping resolves a run's project through its plan, so a run row is only\n  // useful to the sidebar alongside the plan it points at.\n  const streamPlanIds = new Set(runs.map((run) => run.planId).filter(Boolean));\n  const { snapshotChecksum: _snapshotChecksum, ...streamPayload } = payload;\n  return {\n    ...streamPayload,\n    snapshotRunId: selectedRunId,\n    runs,\n    plans: payload.plans.filter((plan) => streamPlanIds.has(plan.id)),"
}
```

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:39:35.012Z

The turn-end trigger stamp is acceptable since it's within the sweep window, but the bigger issue is that an actively running session still sorts by its stale last-turn-end time instead of showing as currently active — which is exactly what the user flagged with a3c8783 sorting below finished sessions despite still spinning.

Given the design intent that only lastActivityAt/createdAt drive Projects tab ordering, I decide not to change that semantics unasked — the frozen catalog was the primary defect, and in practice a3c8783's last_activity_at was recent enough (21:23) that the fix resolves the visible symptom without needing to make working runs float based on worker heartbeat. Now I want to verify the fix compiles and passes the existing tests, including the events-route test that checks source text.

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:40:29.006Z

I realize this breaks an existing test: since the first frame's `deliveredRunPayloads` starts empty, every run counts as "changed," so my change would make the first frame send the full catalog instead of just the selected run. I need to reconsider whether a full first frame is actually desirable, given the client just completed a snapshot bootstrap.

Instead, I'll treat the first frame as having no changes by seeding `deliveredRunPayloads` from the first payload without transmitting it, so deltas only start accumulating afterward — this matches the intended behavior where the client already has the full catalog from bootstrap, and keeps the existing test passing. I'm restructuring the change-detection logic to only compare against `deliveredRunPayloads` when it's been seeded, otherwise treating the changed set as empty.

parent: ddad8db6-48e2-41ed-9225-29dccb205263

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T19:42:42.652Z

The flaky failure looks like a pre-existing issue unrelated to my new test, which passes fine. I'm now checking the client-side merge logic for scoped catalogs—confirming that merged runs prioritize incoming changed rows over current ones for matching ids, and examining the condition that flags when a selected run is absent from a partial catalog update.

With my change, the selected run always stays included when it exists, so that logic is unaffected, and the read markers merge and plans merge both continue using the merged runs correctly. I'll run the client-side state manager and conversations tests as well to double-check everything.

## Claude · 2026-09-06T19:43:22.099Z

Found it, fixed it. Diagnosis:

**The database ordering is correct** — I checked the real values:

| session | last_activity_at |
|---|---|
| a3c8783c2b66 | 21:23 |
| bba9b18f1d55 | 20:28 |
| 90629ba2e9c8 | 18:47 |

So the server has always known a3c8783 is newer. What broke is the *delivery* of that value to the browser.

**The regression** is commit `6591057` (Sep 5, "make event streams efficient and disconnect-safe"). It changed two things at once:

1. `buildEventStreamPayload` trimmed every live SSE frame down to `runs: [selectedRun]` — so while a conversation is open, no other run's row ever reaches the client again.
2. `SNAPSHOT_VALIDATION_INTERVAL_MS` went from 5s to 60s — the full-catalog refresh that used to paper over (1) now runs twelve times less often.

The sidebar sorts each project by `run.lastActivityAt` (`src/lib/conversations.ts:50`). After the change, the run you have open is the only one whose sort key stays current; every other row is frozen until a full snapshot lands. Selecting a different conversation tears down the connection and forces a fresh snapshot, which is why clicking reshuffles the whole list at once and why the session you just clicked always looks newest.

**The fix** (`src/runtime/http/routes/events.ts`): live frames now also carry any *other* run row that changed since the last frame on that connection, diffed per run so a bump on the selected run (which happens on nearly every frame of a live turn) doesn't drag the whole catalog along. Plans for those rows ride with them, since grouping resolves a run's project through its plan. The opening frame is used as the diff baseline rather than a catalog re-send — the client already bootstrapped from a complete snapshot.

Verification: new test in `tests/api/events-route.test.ts` opens a scoped stream, mutates an unselected run's `lastActivityAt`, and asserts the row arrives on a later frame. Full `events-route` suite matches the clean-tree baseline (one pre-existing 8s timeout in "streams run and worker state after conversation session sync" fails both with and without my change). `tsc` is clean apart from a pre-existing error in `tests/app/conversation-transcript-retention.test.ts`.

Restart the runner (pid 31478) to pick this up.

One behaviour I did *not* change, since it predates the breakage: `lastActivityAt` only advances on a user message or a **finished** agent turn. A session that has been mid-turn for an hour keeps the key from its last completed turn, so it can sit below a session that just wrapped up — even with its spinner going. Say the word if you want active work to count toward the sort.

parent: 2119b10b-581e-43ed-8544-6ad8f3e52dbb

## Claude thinking · 2026-09-06T22:53:21.034Z

I'm considering that this detection only catches failures lacking a string error code, so a genuine `RuntimeApiError` with something like an HTTP 502/503 code would still register as a real failure rather than a transport dropout. I'm now tracing through what actually happens when the user restarts the local runner on the same origin, since the restart endpoint kills the process mid-request.

A plain fetch rejecting with "Failed to fetch" gets swallowed correctly since it has no code, but if the shutting-down server replies with a graceful 503 before dying, that surfaces as a coded `RuntimeApiError` and shows a generic error instead — which seems like the likely culprit here. I recall the runtime HTTP server test describing a shutdown sequence that announces shutdown, flushes briefly, then cancels open readers, so I want to check how the restart-control flow interacts with that shutdown path on the client side.
